Properly fixing that cosmetic edge means signalling "this departure is a jetpack rescue, not a real fall" over the wire, which is new scope, not this bug.
- **Verified**: via `?solo=1&role=player&roster=a,b,c&myToken=b&forks=RR` (new dev-only params, App.jsx — see its own comment) plus `window.__figurePose()`/`window.__testUpdateTeammate()`/`window.__teammates()`. Confirmed: resting seat offset matches the expected table value exactly (token `b`, index 1 of 3 → `+ISLAND_SPACING_X`); the walking figure's lateral offset shrinks from that full value toward 0 as `leg.traveled` grows, consistent with the `SEAT_TAPER_DIST` formula; on arrival the offset returns to the SAME fixed value at the new fork; two simulated teammates (`a` at index 0 → centre, `c` at index 2 → `-ISLAND_SPACING_X`) land at their correct fixed offsets regardless of arrival order. `npm run build` clean. **Not yet verified with a real multi-device test** — same standing gap as everywhere else in this file; this needs at least two real people to actually watch each other walk at an angle and see the jetpack-rescue fix's "no longer invisible" outcome live.
- **Still open, unaffected by this change**: Watch mode's own camera-FOLLOW behaviour (as opposed to layout, which this entry covers) still rides the guide's shared `isSpectatorRole`/`guideMode` state machine — that was always a separate piece of work from seating (see goal 3 above) and nothing here changes it.

### ✓ Built 2026-09-13: tag alternation bug fix, mesh-based name tags, per-player randomised bridge sides
- **Three separate fixes from one message, done together**:
  1. **Tag above/below alternation was broken by the fixed-seating work above.** `tagSide` was being assigned by raw roster-array index parity, not by final on-screen left-to-right order — roster index 0..4 sits at screen positions centre,+1,-1,+2,-2, completely uncorrelated with left-to-right, so two screen-adjacent players could both land "above" and overlap. Luke: "always start with the left player... above... then to the right... below... then to the right... above." Fixed by sorting the seat table by `offsetX` before assigning `tagSide`, so alternation now follows true screen order, starting `above` at the leftmost seat. Verified with a 5-seat roster: sorted left-to-right order landed above/below/above/below/above exactly as specified, self included at whatever seat it happened to land.
  2. **Name tags rebuilt as meshes parented to the rig, not a screen-space DOM overlay.** Luke: "the names should be affixed to the character card, so that they have the same facing and move with the character at all times... in the current build [they] shift relative to the camera, turning to face it." Checked git history for the "previously solved" mesh version Luke recalled — it was never actually a shipped/committed state (both the mesh attempt and the DOM rewrite happened within developing the same original name-tag feature, 2026-08-30/31) — so this is a fresh build, not a literal revert, but one that fixes the exact bug that sank the original attempt: "below" used to be computed by negating "above"'s head-relative formula, which measures from the wrong reference point and lands slightly UNDER the ground plane (invisible/occluded by solid terrain) regardless of how small the magnitude is. Fixed by measuring "below" up from the feet instead of down from the head — see `tagLocalY()`'s own comment. A tag is now a real `THREE.Mesh` (canvas texture, same `buildNameTagCanvas` pipeline as before) added as a child of `targetRig.group`, inheriting its position AND rotation for free — no per-frame projection math needed at all (word signs already prove this reads correctly on screen, since the camera only ever views the scene from a consistent relative angle). Visibility is now a plain `mesh.visible` flag instead of a DOM `display` toggle. Removed the now-dead `#nameTagLayer` DOM layer and its CSS from `chrome.js`. **One real behaviour change, flagged rather than hidden**: a real mesh is now properly occluded by intervening geometry (fog, pillars, etc.), unlike the old always-on-top DOM overlay — and "below" necessarily sits closer to the character's own base (just above the ground) rather than hanging fully past the feet, since a real mesh can't clip through solid terrain the way a flat screen overlay could.
  3. **Correct bridge side is now randomised per player, independently.** Luke: "the correct side/bridge and the matching word needs to be randomised per player... we need a way to prevent players from seeing which choice their teammates made." Feasible cleanly because every device already builds its entire 3D world independently from shared round data — there's no server-authoritative scene to keep in sync. Split what used to be one shared decision into two: the word PAIR and which of its two members is correct stay shared (broadcast once, since everyone must agree on what word the guide actually said) — `wordPairs.js`'s `assignForkWords` now returns unassigned `{a, b}` pairs instead of pre-flipped `{left, right}`. Which physical bridge each member sits on is now a fresh, LOCAL `Math.random()` in `buildFork()` (skyPath.js), done independently on every device, every fork; `sec.correct` (physical side) is derived locally from that flip against the shared "which member is correct" signal. Every existing use of `sec.correct`/`sec.words` was already per-device-local to begin with (checked every call site), so nothing downstream needed to change. This closes the leak at the source: watching a teammate walk toward "the left bridge" now conveys nothing, since your own left/right mapping is uncorrelated with theirs — even watching their bridge crumble in real time doesn't help, since "left was wrong for them" says nothing about your own left. **Left the fog-stand-in system as-is, per Luke's explicit instruction** — the possible simplification flagged when this was proposed (retiring the fake stand-in now that the side-leak it protected against no longer exists) was NOT done; revisit only if asked.
- **Verified**: `npm run build` clean; `window.__sections()` after forcing `?forks=LLLRRR` (a fixed shared "which member" sequence) showed a `correct` sequence that did NOT match `LLLRRR` one-to-one — confirming the physical side is now genuinely independent of the shared signal — while forcing a choice on the freshly-read `sec.correct` still walked cleanly to the next island with no fall, confirming each device's own win/lose check stays internally consistent regardless. Tag alternation verified via simulated 5-member roster (`?roster=a,b,c,d,e&myToken=c`) landing in correct sorted above/below order. No console errors in any of these sessions. **Not yet verified with a real multi-device test** — same standing gap as the entry above.
- **Follow-up, still needed — deferred**: the "below" name tag placement (item 2 above) still needs more work per Luke, 2026-09-13, after seeing it live: it was flickering (a real z-fight against the character's own card mesh — both sitting on the same local Z plane, overlapping where "below" sat within the card's own height) and visually covering the avatar's feet. First fix applied same day: nudged the "below" tag forward (`NAME_TAG_BELOW_Z`, a real mesh-local Z offset, not just a Y change) off the card's own plane entirely, landing it on the open ground just in front of the feet, matching a reference screenshot Luke provided. This resolves the immediate complaint but Luke's message flagged more work is wanted here later — treat the current placement as a working first pass, not settled final numbers.

### ✓ Built 2026-09-13: real fall/jetpack visibility, "potentially visible to everyone"
- **The ask**: Luke, continuing the same conversation: "when a player falls, I want that to be visible on everyone's screen (even if the camera doesn't focus on it). Similarly, when a player uses the jetpack, I want that to be visible for everyone... if a player chooses the wrong bridge, the guide should only see them fall once they've actually fallen, and they should see the proper falling animation, albeit from their own POV." Clarified after I flagged tension with "leave the fog system as-is": "potentially visible" means the underlying data reaches every device's system regardless of role — it does not mean every viewer's render path must actually show it. Fog stays exactly as it was for a PLAYER viewer; only the SPECTATOR (guide, Watch mode) rendering path was upgraded.
- **Mechanism — one unified extension of the already-proven pattern**, not two separate systems: the same throttled 'moving' ping that already streams a walking player's real x/z/heading (see the earlier live-tracking entry) now ALSO fires during a real physics fall (`tick()`'s falling block, from the live Rapier `fallBody` transform) and during a jetpack rescue (`updateRescue()`, from `figure.position`/`figure.quaternion`, which that function already computed as a pure function of `rescue.t` — nothing new to derive, just broadcast what was already being drawn locally). Both now send the richer `{x, y, z, quat}` shape instead of the walking case's `{x, z, heading}`; `updateTeammates()`'s spectator branch checks for `.quat` and, when present, drives the teammate rig's real position AND full 3D rotation (lerp + slerp) instead of the old X/Z-plus-heading-only walking case.
- **Why the causality requirement ("only see them fall once they've actually fallen") falls out for free**: the broadcast fires at the exact same instant the real local event starts (`startFall()`/`startRescue()`'s own call sites), so there is no advance warning built into the design at all — the only delay a spectator sees is ordinary network latency, which Luke already said is fine ("it can be a little stilted").
- **The old fall stand-in (sink-and-tip) is now a fallback, not the normal path.** Before this, there was no explicit "I am falling" signal at all — a spectator inferred a fall only once pings went silent, then played a fully synthetic cosmetic in place of the real thing (this was the ONLY option at the time). Now that a real transform stream usually exists, `entry.hadRealTransform` (set the first time a real quat-carrying ping arrives) tells `updateTeammates()` to just vanish the rig once those real pings finally go stale, rather than replaying the synthetic sink-and-tip on top of a tumble that already played for real. The synthetic stand-in still exists purely as a safety net for the rare case no real data ever arrived (e.g. a lost packet right at the start of a fall).
- **Jetpack needed no separate "vanish" handling at all** — a rescue already ends with an explicit 'resting' report (the bug fixed earlier this same day), so the real-transform pings simply stop at the same moment that clean arrival report fires; no staleness/fallback logic needed for it.
- **Added `window.__lastPlayerState`**, a small permanent debug hook (mirrors the existing `window.__teammates()` receiving-side view) recording the most recent outgoing `notifyPlayerState()` call, since solo/dev mode has no real network hop to inspect otherwise.
- **Verified (but see the real bug found right after, below)**: forced a real wrong-choice fall (`?solo=1&role=player`) and confirmed `window.__lastPlayerState` carried a real `{x, y, z, quat}` triple matching the live Rapier body, not a placeholder. Did the same for a real jetpack rescue (via the dev `addJetpack` button) — confirmed live quat-carrying pings throughout the whole rescue animation, followed by a clean final `{phase: 'resting'}` report once it finished. Separately drove a simulated teammate through `window.__testUpdateTeammate` and confirmed a spectator's tracked copy lerped/slerped to the exact real position and orientation given. `npm run build` clean. **What this testing missed**: every check above sent a 'moving' ping IMMEDIATELY after 'departing', with no real gap in between — which is exactly backwards from real play, where a player taps their choice (firing 'departing' right away) and then doesn't necessarily start actually holding forward to walk for a beat afterward. That gap is precisely what the bug below lived in, and no debug-hook script that always follows 'departing' with an instant 'moving' ping could ever have exercised it — a real lesson on the value of a live human tester over a scripted one, not a caching/timing fluke.

- **✗ Broken as shipped, ✓ fixed same day.** Luke, live: "when the player chose the wrong bridge without a backpack, they immediately fell through the current island in the guide's view, before they had moved at all. Similar for the jetpack: the player disappears until they have completed the sequence... at which point they suddenly appear on the next island... there is nothing close to real-time tracking." Root cause, found by re-reading `updateTeammates()`'s spectator branch rather than guessing: `fresh` (whether `entry.livePos` is recent) is ALSO false in the ordinary gap right after 'departing' fires — before a real player has even started holding forward, let alone sent a first position ping — and the code had no way to tell that gap apart from "pings genuinely stopped because they fell." So the willFall/hadRealTransform fallbacks fired on THAT gap instead: a wrong choice showed the sink-and-tip stand-in starting instantly, at the ORIGINAL fork, before any walking was ever shown ("fell through the current island... before they had moved") — and since a jetpack save ALSO sets `willFall` true (it's set from `!wasCorrect`, before the game even knows a jetpack will intervene), the exact same premature stand-in hid a jetpack-bound player's rig entirely, well before their real rescue transform pings could ever be seen, explaining "disappears until they've completed the sequence... then suddenly appears." This bug PRE-DATES this session's changes (the exact same premature-trigger condition already existed in the original live-tracking work), just newly exposed by finally being watched over a real gap instead of a scripted one.
  - **Fix**: gate both fallbacks (the old stand-in and the new real-transform vanish) behind `entry.livePosAt > 0` — "has at least one real ping ever arrived since this departure began." `livePosAt` is already reset to 0 the instant 'departing' fires and only ever set once a genuine 'moving' ping lands, so this needed no new field, just using an existing one correctly. Before the first real ping, the teammate now simply holds at the fork (same as the final `else` case already did for "pings went stale and it's not a fall") — closing the gap the bug lived in without touching the sending side at all.
  - **Verified**: simulated a departure with `willFall: true` and NO follow-up ping for 800ms (four times `LIVE_POS_STALE_MS`) — position stayed exactly at the resting fork the whole time, no premature sink. Then sent a real walking ping (position tracked correctly), then a real quat-carrying fall ping (position/rotation tracked correctly), then stopped — position correctly settled and held at the last real transform once stale, ready to vanish via the (now correctly reachable) `hadRealTransform` path. `npm run build` clean. **Still not verified with a real multi-device test** — same standing gap as everywhere else in this file, and the one type of test that would have caught the original bug before it shipped.
  - **✗ Still broken after that fix too, per Luke, live** ("I don't see any difference... they appear to immediately fall... no tracking of their movement... the character similarly disappears (falls through the island) as soon as they make the incorrect choice"). Two more observations from him that changed the approach: it happens on PLAYERS' screens as well as the guide's — so it was never a spectator-branch problem alone — and the whole thing had become a pile of inference (a fixed fog stand-in that starts the instant a choice is made; a stand-in fall triggered by silence; a vanish triggered by silence) with too many ways to show an event that hasn't happened. His instruction: stop verifying my own changes (scripted checks kept passing while real play kept failing — the scripted ones never had a real gap between choosing and walking), make the changes as a series of steps, and he checks each live.

### Rebuilt 2026-09-13: explicit-only movement reporting, one code path for every viewer, fog hidden — in progress, step by step, each checked live by Luke
- **Luke's spec, verbatim, the acceptance bar**: "all character movements (successfully crossing the bridge, falling, using the jetpack, being abducted by aliens) should be in theory viewable by other players, even though they are at times blocked, by fog or perhaps not being within the field of vision... if a player chooses the wrong path, nothing at all should happen from the perspective of either the guide or their teammates: once they choose the path, they don't move. They only move once they press the forward arrow. Everyone else will then see them move forward, only as far as they actually move: if they stop moving, by taking their finger off the move forward button, everyone else will see them stop. No one will see them fall until and unless they walk all the way to the mid-point of the bridge, which triggers the fall." Also: hide the fog entirely for now (don't delete), and drop the "walk straight into the fog" stand-in for good — players see teammates' true movement now.
- **What changed (skyPath.js)**: the phase model is now four EXPLICIT reports from the moving device and nothing else — 'resting', 'departing' (rig holds still until real positions arrive), 'moving' (the real transform, `{x,z,heading}` walking or `{x,y,z,quat}` falling/rescued/abducted, throttled at MOVING_PING_INTERVAL, sent for as long as the thing is actually happening), and a new 'gone' (sent once when a fall's card has vanished or an abduction has carried them off; hides the copy). `updateTeammates()` is one branch for every role: smooth toward the latest `livePos` if there is one, otherwise do nothing. DELETED: the fog stand-in walk, the silence-triggered sink-and-tip, the silence-triggered vanish, `willFall` on the wire, `LIVE_POS_STALE_MS`, `FOG_DEPART_DURATION`, `FALL_STANDIN_DURATION`, and the `everMoved` gate from the failed fix above — every path that could show something the sender hadn't reported. An abduction now announces 'departing' itself (it starts from rest, not a fork choice) and streams like a fall. Fog curtains: `FOG_CURTAINS_VISIBLE = false` in makeCurtain — built and animated as before, just not drawn. A temporary diagnostic line per teammate in the on-screen HUD (phase · messages received · time since last · what kind of position) so a live test can be read straight off the screen.
- **Deliberately NOT verified by me** — Luke's instruction. Build is clean; nothing else claimed.
- **Steps, one at a time, each one checked live by Luke before the next — IN PROGRESS, this is the authoritative status if a session ends mid-way**:
  1. **✓ Passed, 2026-09-14.** Wrong choice with no jetpack, a second device watching: nothing happens on the watcher's screen until forward is pressed; movement then matches, stops when released; the fall appears only at the bridge midpoint, as a real tumble; the card then vanishes. Confirmed live by Luke ("Good, that's working now").
  2. **Next up, not yet started.** Same with a jetpack: the real rescue flight visible on the watcher, ending standing at the next island. Uses the exact same `pingFigureTransform()` mechanism step 1 already proved out (see updateRescue's own call to it) — expected to work, but per Luke's explicit instruction NOT to be marked done until he's watched it live himself; don't skip the check just because the mechanism is shared.
  3. Correct choice: real walk across, ending seated at the next island (the seat-walk diagonal included).
  4. Abduction, watched from another device.
  5. Guide camera follow behaviour through each of the above (it reads the same rig transforms, so should just work — but check).
  6. **Fog re-enabled 2026-09-14** (`FOG_CURTAINS_VISIBLE = true`), once Luke confirmed steps 1-5 (every movement type, plus guide camera-follow through all of them, including the abduction-anchor seat-offset bug found and fixed along the way): "most cam/follow issues now seem to be fixed. Turn the fog back on." Not yet confirmed that the fog correctly OCCLUDES the now-real movement rather than something being left over from the old stand-in system — worth a look now that it's back on. **HUD diagnostic line deliberately NOT removed yet** — Luke only asked for the fog; "most... issues" leaves open that something could still need the diagnostic, so it stays until he says the tracking work itself is fully settled (search skyPath.js for "Temporary diagnostic (2026-09-13)" when that time comes).
- **If this takes longer than expected / gets picked up cold in a later session**: the full context is the two entries directly above this one (the two failed attempts) plus this one — read all three before touching the code again, since the two failures both looked like they should have worked and weren't caught by any amount of self-testing, only by Luke actually playing it. Do not re-introduce timing-based inference (staleness timeouts, "assume they fell if I haven't heard from them") anywhere in this system — that pattern is what caused both prior failures, and the explicit-report-only model this entry replaced it with is deliberate, not incidental.

### ✓ Built 2026-09-14: "Dev player" button in the lobby (Lobby.jsx)
- **Ask**: Luke — opening several browser tabs and manually resetting identity + typing a name in each, for multiplayer testing, was slow. One button: reset identity, pick the next unused name from a fixed list (Serena, William, Louis, Faraday, Theresa, Wilhelmina), join.
- **First attempt was wrong**: picked the next name by checking `lobby.participants` (the `useLobby` hook's own state) — but that's only ever populated once THIS device has joined the session's channel at least once; a brand-new tab that's never joined has an empty view of the room. Luke: "it doesn't check for the name and use the next name on the list: all six tabs used 'Serena'" — exactly that gap, hit for real with six fresh tabs.
- **Fix**: `fetchCurrentDisplayNames(sessionCode)` (useLobby.js, exported standalone) opens a short-lived, throwaway presence-only channel on the SAME lobby channel a real join would use, reads the first presence sync (who's actually there right now, from Supabase directly, independent of this device's own state), then tears itself down — never calls `.track()`, so it never announces itself as a participant. `handleDevPlayer` in Lobby.jsx awaits this before picking a name, with a "Checking…" disabled state on the button while it does.
- **Verified**: three genuinely separate fresh browser tabs (each navigated fresh, never sharing state) correctly picked Serena, William, Louis in sequence — the exact scenario that broke before. No console errors.
- **Known limitation, by design, not fixed**: the button reads who's *currently connected*. If a tab that took a dev name is closed/reset, that name frees up immediately (Supabase presence drops on clean disconnect) and a later click can hand it back out. For N simultaneous dev players, open N tabs and click once per tab — don't click repeatedly in one tab expecting names to accumulate, since each click abandons the previous identity.

### ✓ Built 2026-09-14: jetpack visible remotely, lands at the correct seat, loop removed
Three related fixes from one message, after Luke's step-2 live check of the jetpack found real problems:
- **1. No jetpack visible to other viewers ("only the moving avatar").** Root cause: `rig.powerup` (the backpack card, clip, flame, smoke) was ALWAYS built on the LOCAL player's own `rig` (`equipPowerUp(rig)`), and never networked at all — a teammate's copy in the `teammates` map had no idea a jetpack existed. Turned out every power-up function (`equipPowerUp`/`startEngineFlame`/`stopEngineFlame`/`updateEngineFlame`/`updateEngineSmoke`) already took an arbitrary `rig` parameter rather than closing over the local one, so they work unmodified on a teammate's rig. Fix: `notifyPlayerState()` now includes `hasJetpack`/`firing` (cheap booleans read off `rig.powerup` on every existing report — no new event needed) and `updateTeammate()` mirrors them onto the teammate's own rig by calling the exact same equip/dispose/flame functions. `updateTeammates()` now also drives `updateEngineFlame`/`updateEngineSmoke` for every teammate each frame (own local frame-cycling, not networked frame-by-frame, same as the local player's). The equip button also nudges an immediate report, since equipping usually happens while standing still (no other report would fire for a while otherwise). Factored the geometry/material disposal duplicated across `disposeRig`/`resolveRescue`/`restart()` into one `disposePowerUp(rig)`, needed for the teammate-side disposal anyway.
- **2. Landing in the centre, then jumping to the correct seat.** Root cause: the rescue's `landing` target was always the bare fork centre (`sec.nextCursor`, no seat offset) — the WHOLE flight animation flew to and touched down at that centre, and only the NEXT frame's ordinary resting render (`currentSeatLateral()`) applied this player's fixed seat, snapping them sideways the instant the sequence ended. Fixed at the source: `startRescue()` now bakes this player's own seat offset into `landing` itself (skipped for the last fork, which has no seating concept — same reasoning `currentSeatLateral()` already uses for a leg with no `arriveFork`). Every phase of the flight already aims at `landing`, so nothing else needed to change — and since this is also what's broadcast via `pingFigureTransform()`, remote viewers get the correct landing spot for free too. **Important adjacent fix**: `resolveRescue()` used to set `walker` (the pure-centreline simulation position bridge/fall math depends on) directly from `landing` — now that `landing` carries a seat offset, that would have permanently shifted the walker off centreline for the rest of the round. Fixed to use `sec.nextCursor` (still pure centre) for `walker`, keeping `landing`'s seat offset purely a rendering/animation target, exactly the same separation of concerns the whole seat-walk system already relies on elsewhere.
- **3. Removed the loop, straight flight instead.** Luke, after watching it live: "I've decided I'd like to remove the loop the player does: have them fly straight to the point above the next island and then descend. This should slightly reduce the total time of the animation as well." Collapsed the old three-part flight (sideways ignition burst → looping arc back over the island) into one straight `flyDuration` leg, fall's end to directly above the landing spot, then the existing straight-down descent — `rescuePosAt`/`updateRescue` phase count dropped from 6 to 5. `flyOutDistance`/`flyOutRise`/`loopRadius` (the sideways-burst target and the loop's own radius) no longer mean anything and were deleted, along with their `?rescueTune=1` sliders. New `flyDuration` defaults to 3.4s (was flyOut 1.9s + arc 2.8s = 4.7s combined) — a first-pass number, NOT yet Luke-tuned; the `?rescueTune=1` panel (with the "Test Reset" button for replaying the sequence without a page reload) is still the right place to dial it in live.
- **Verified (data/logic level only, per Luke's standing instruction not to rely on my own verification for whether a feature actually plays correctly)**: `npm run build` clean. Forced a real wrong-choice rescue with `?roster=a,b,c&myToken=b` (seat offset +1.35) — `window.__rescue().landing` showed `x: 1.3505` (the correct seat, not 0/centre) from the very first frame of the flight, and the figure settled at exactly that same X once landed, with `hasJetpack` correctly flipping back to `false` in the broadcast state right after. No console errors. **This still needs Luke's own live two-device look** — same as every step in the movement-tracking rebuild above, and doubly so here since the flight's own tuning (`flyDuration`, `apexHeight`, etc.) is a first pass, not settled numbers.

### ✓ Built 2026-09-14: the detached engine's fly-off is now visible remotely too
- **Gap found by Luke, live, right after the previous fix**: "we don't see the jetpack leaving the screen from the POV of another player or the guide. After the player lands, the jetpack simply disappears." Cause: `hasJetpack`/`firing` (the previous fix) only covered "does a teammate have a backpack, is it currently firing" — the LOCAL detach-and-fly-off flourish (`updateRescue`'s `scene.attach` + upward animation once the hold phase ends) was never signalled at all, so a remote viewer's copy just got disposed outright the instant the 'resting' arrival report turned `hasJetpack` false.
- **Fix**: `notifyPlayerState` now also sends `detached` (`!!rescue?.detached`, the same flag `updateRescue` already tracks locally), riding the same already-frequent 'moving' pings sent throughout a rescue — no new event type. On the receiving end, `updateTeammate` reacts to `detached` turning true by doing exactly what the local original does — `scene.attach(entry.rig.powerup.group)` (re-parenting it off the character, preserving its current world transform) and recording where it was at that moment — then `updateTeammates()` advances a fixed-duration (`RESCUE_TUNE.departDuration`/`departDistance`, the same shared constants) upward animation each frame, timed from whenever THIS device first saw the flag (there's no continuous position stream for the departing engine itself, just the one trigger), disposing the power-up itself once that duration elapses. The earlier immediate-dispose-on-`hasJetpack:false` path now checks whether a depart animation is already running and, if so, leaves it alone to finish and dispose itself — otherwise a 'resting' report landing before the local depart timer completes would cut the fly-off short.
- **Verified (data/logic level, per Luke's standing instruction)**: simulated the full sequence via `window.__testUpdateTeammate` — equip, fire, detach, ~1.5s of ticking through the depart duration, a late redundant `hasJetpack:false` report — no console errors, no crash, no double-disposal. `npm run build` clean. **Needs Luke's own live look, same as everything else in this thread** — no debug hook currently exposes a teammate's own powerup transform to confirm the fly-off actually reads correctly on screen, only that nothing breaks running the sequence.

### ✓ Built 2026-09-14: step 4 (abduction) networked — a local replica, not a stream
- **Different shape of problem from the jetpack.** The jetpack's backpack is a small prop that just rides along with the character's real (already-streamed) position — trivial to mirror remotely. An abduction's saucer/beam are NOT passive: `alienAbduction.js`'s single `apply()` function drives the ship's position AND the character card's position together as one scripted ensemble (random entry/exit slant angles rolled once at `start()`, everything else a pure function of elapsed time). Streaming the real transform (like the fall/rescue) would fight a receiver's own copy of that ensemble rather than usefully feed it.
- **Fix**: `notifyPlayerState` now also sends `abducting` (`!!abduction`), meaningful only on the 'departing' report `startAbduction()` already sends. A receiver seeing `abducting: true` builds its OWN `buildAbduction(...)` instance (same factory the local game already uses — it was already written to take an arbitrary target, never assuming it's the local player) anchored at wherever that teammate was actually seated, and lets it fully own that rig's position/rotation via its own `update(dt)`/`facePoint(camera.position)` each frame — the existing lerp-toward-broadcast-position code is skipped entirely while a teammate's abduction instance is active. Disposed once its own local `state.playing` goes false, with defensive disposal also on an explicit 'gone' or a later 'resting' report, so nothing can leak or linger into a future round.
- **Known, accepted imprecision**: since `entryAngle`/`exitAngle` are randomly rolled independently on every device (the abducted player's own screen AND every viewer's own local replica), the saucer's approach/departure slant will look slightly different from each vantage — not a perfect replay, just "a UFO abducts them, convincingly, from here." Timing (durations, hover height, bob) is identical everywhere since none of that is randomized.
- **Verified (data/logic level, per Luke's standing instruction)**: simulated a teammate going resting → departing-with-`abducting:true` → several seconds of ticking (mid-flight) → an EARLY 'gone' report arriving before the local replica had finished on its own — confirmed the mid-flight dispose path runs with no console errors, no crash. `npm run build` clean. **Needs Luke's own live two-device look** — same as every step in this thread; I have no way to confirm the saucer actually reads correctly on a second screen from here, only that nothing breaks running the sequence.

### ✓ Fixed 2026-09-14/15: a player's OWN name tag could be on the wrong side (above vs below) for the whole round
- **Symptom (Luke, live, repeatedly)**: a position-4 player (assigned "below") arriving on an island with their name ABOVE their head — only ever in that player's own view, never the guide's, sometimes from the very first island. Every data dump (`window.__seatOffsets()`, `window.__teammates()`) showed the assignment itself was correct, which is what finally pointed away from the seat table.
- **Root cause, found with a temporary event log (`window.__tagDebugLog()`, still in place)**: `finishCharacterSelect` called `attachNameTag(rig, ...)` — an ASYNC canvas build, defaulting to `side: 'above'` — and then immediately `setNameTagSide(rig, mySeat.tagSide)` to fix it. That fix-up ran before the tag mesh existed and silently no-opped; the async build then created the mesh at 'above'. A TEAMMATE's tag recovers because `relayoutAllIslands` re-checks it every frame; a device's own tag has no such recheck, so it stayed wrong all round. Fix: `attachNameTag` takes `side`/`yStagger` up front (new `yStagger` opt) and the racy follow-up call is gone. Confirmed by Luke live ("that finally seems to have fixed it").
- Alongside it: the seat table is now five FIXED slots (index → above/below/above/below/above; a smaller group takes the middle slots — see `seatOffsets`), per Luke's own design; and below-tag placement got a `?tagTune=1` panel, values locked in (`NAME_TAG_BELOW_Y_OFFSET` 0.21, `NAME_TAG_BELOW_Z` 1.06) with the panel deliberately kept live while `NAME_TAG_HEIGHT` (−10%) settles.

Attacker gets `showNotice()` with the outcome. `window.__testGameEvent(kind, payload)` simulates any of it solo. ### ✓ Built 2026-09-15: the abduction device's real art, attached like the jetpack; island-2 icons now match the attached card
- **Device art**: Luke's `Device.png`, already composited onto the same oval cardboard the jetpack's engine cards use (no separate blank-oval asset exists anywhere in the project to composite onto programmatically — checked, see the earlier TODO entry). Saved as `Assets/Abduction Device.png` (source) and `app/public/textures/abduct-device.png`.
- **Generalised the power-up system to a second kind, not a parallel one.** The abduction trigger used to be a small corner badge (`rig.abductBadge`), separate from the jetpack's clip-mounted card (`rig.powerup`) — Luke asked for it "as close as you possibly can to the way the jetpack is attached," so it's now the SAME mechanism: `equipPowerUp(rig, kind)` picks its frame table from a new `POWERUP_FRAMES = { jetpack: ENGINE_FRAMES, abduction: ABDUCT_DEVICE_FRAMES }` (the abduction one a single static frame, scale picked to match the jetpack's own idle-frame rendered height), and `rig.powerup.kind` says which. One consequence caught while doing this: the wrong-bridge fall/rescue branch used to check bare `if (rig.powerup) startRescue()` — true for EITHER item once they share a slot — now correctly checks `rig.powerup?.kind === 'jetpack'`, or holding the abduction device would have wrongly granted a jetpack rescue. The wire protocol collapsed the same way: `hasJetpack`/`hasAbductTrigger` booleans → one `powerupKind` (`'jetpack' | 'abduction' | null`), teammates synced from one block instead of two. `restart()` now unconditionally clears whatever's equipped (previously only cleared a jetpack if `rescue` was active — a pre-existing gap, fixed in passing).
- **Island-2 icons, Luke: "the same images used when they are attached to players... 80% of the size... spin about their vertical central axis, revealing their backs which look the same but reversed. Period of 360 degree rotation, 2s."** `buildPickup()` now reads `POWERUP_FRAMES[kind][0]` and the same `frameWorldSize()` maths `applyPowerUpTune` uses for the attached card (factored out so a future retune of either card's size resizes its island icon for free), scaled by `PICKUP_SCALE = 0.8`. No clip (never had one). Spin is `pickupMesh.rotation.y = baseHeading + t * (2π / 2s)` — the "reversed back" is free: a double-sided plane shows its front texture mirrored when viewed from behind, which for a centred, front-to-back-symmetric oval card is indistinguishable from a real reversed backside.
- **Verified**: solo, both kinds — device renders correctly clipped to the card (screenshotted, matches the jetpack's own attachment point/angle), island icon visible spinning above island 2 at the smaller size, claim → equip → `#useAbduct` enables, no console errors on a fresh tab. Jetpack path re-checked end to end (attach, screenshot) to confirm the generalisation didn't regress it. **Not yet Luke's own live look** at the actual device art size/position or the spin's readability.

### In progress 2026-09-16: the cardboard UI — a shared cardboard panel with things that sit on it (dial + housing; alien device interface). Started during the pause on the abduction-targeting feature.
**If Luke says "the cardboard UI", "move the dial around", "the alien interface", or "the abduction device UI": start at [app/src/dialProto.js](app/src/dialProto.js) — its header comment is the full design doc. THE REAL IN-GAME VERSION now lives in `app/src/skypath/skyPath.js`'s own "abduction cardboard UI" section (search for that heading) — everything below this line is the PROTOTYPE history that built up to it; the prototypes themselves are untouched and still work as their own reference/comparison pages. The pieces, all separate on purpose ("this will be one use for the cardboard background, not the only use"): [app/src/cardboardPanel.js](app/src/cardboardPanel.js) is the shared backing panel (+ its string-hole keep-clear data); [app/dial-tuner.html](app/dial-tuner.html) + [app/src/dialProto.js](app/src/dialProto.js) are the dial + housing; [app/alien-interface.html](app/alien-interface.html) + [app/src/alienInterfaceProto.js](app/src/alienInterfaceProto.js) are the alien device screen that grows onto the panel; [app/alien-lower.html](app/alien-lower.html) + [app/src/alienLowerProto.js](app/src/alienLowerProto.js) are the panel + strings + growing screen staged as ONE in-game moment (lowered from off-screen on a button press, filling most of the viewport, in front of everything); [app/alien-target-picker.html](app/alien-target-picker.html) + [app/src/alienTargetPickerProto.js](app/src/alienTargetPickerProto.js) are the actual CONTENT of the grown screen — the avatar/name-tag/arrows for picking a target; [app/alien-fizzle-tuner.html](app/alien-fizzle-tuner.html) + [app/src/alienFizzleProto.js](app/src/alienFizzleProto.js) are six candidate "avatar appearing" effects, side by side, for Luke to pick one from — NOT wired into the target picker yet. dialProto.js's own header comment is the full design doc (the malleable-position idea, exactly how the alpha-compositing/anchor-point mechanism works, what's baked in vs still a live control, and what's not built yet). Read that header before touching any of it; this TODO entry is only a pointer, not a substitute.**
- **What it is, briefly**: a cardboard sign lowered on string from the top of the screen (the lowering itself not built yet), carrying dials/lettering whose position changes screen to screen depending what information that screen needs — Luke: "this is going to be a malleable interface that we will use in the future, with the dial and its housing moved to different areas. The position will depend on what kind of screen I want with what information." This prototype is the reference implementation of ONE dial: a housing (window + dial hole, with a real drop shadow) alpha-blended onto the backing at any position, with the dial locked to a fixed offset from the housing so it always follows correctly.
- **Real bug found and fixed along the way**: `Assets/Cardboard Panel.png`'s PNG colour-key transparency (pure black = "transparent") is NOT reliably honoured by the browser — it rendered as solid opaque black. Fixed by regenerating it with a genuine per-pixel alpha channel. Worth checking for on any OTHER cardboard art that looks transparent in an editor but renders solid black in the browser.
- **Settled and baked into dialProto.js as constants (Luke, live)**: the dial's offset within the housing (centred), its size relative to the hole (~0.92), and the turn duration (0.25s, down from a 0.2-0.8s tuning range) — none of these are sliders any more. Housing position (`housingX`/`housingY`) is the one thing still exposed, deliberately: it's the one thing meant to keep changing screen to screen.
- **Built**: the alpha-blended three-layer composite (backing/housing/dial); a 45°-per-click turn, either direction, eased, at the locked duration; `window.__dialState()` debug hook (the dial art itself is a plain featureless disc right now, so a turn isn't visible by eye without it).
- **Open**: everything under "NOT BUILT YET" in dialProto.js's header — the string-lowering animation, the window's white paper + changing printed values, lettering on the cardboard, more than one dial/layout actually wired up, any real trigger for a turn, and the eventual connection back to the alien abduction feature this was commissioned for (see the "Paused 2026-09-15" entry above).
- **Added 2026-09-16, the alien device interface** (`app/alien-interface.html` + `app/src/alienInterfaceProto.js`): the alien screen appearing ON the cardboard panel, from a marked-up reference Luke drew (green box = roughly where the finished screen sits, red bar = roughly the shape it starts from). **The growth is two sequential steps, at Luke's own ask**: the starting line first WIDENS to full width at its starting height ("spread wide first while still remaining at the same height"), then OPENS vertically to the finished screen. Each step has its own duration slider, plus an optional hold between them (default 0). **Final size and place are BAKED IN** from the values Luke settled on live — 860 x 438 at x 86-946, y 93-531, held in code as width 860 + centre (516, 312) so the height stays derived from the art's own 1024x522 aspect and can never be stretched; those sliders are gone. Remaining sliders: start width/height, widen duration, hold, open duration, plus play/loop/reset. The hole-clearance warning is kept as a guard in case the baked constants are ever edited (it can't trigger at the current values — top edge clears the holes by ~8px).
- **The split matters**: `cardboardPanel.js` was added as the shared backing so uses stack on top of it without touching each other — Luke: "use the UI we just worked on, but don't alter its base code, as it will be used elsewhere without this addition." `dialProto.js` was deliberately NOT changed and still carries its own copy of the panel constants; switching it to import from `cardboardPanel.js` is a pure no-behaviour-change refactor available whenever it's wanted, not done unasked.
- **Step 2 REVEALS rather than stretches** (Luke, same day): "rather than stretch the image vertically, for the second step, would it be possible to have the height extension rather reveal the image?" So step 1 still squashes the art into the widening bar (explicitly fine by him), but step 2 draws the art at its FINAL size and opens a clip aperture over it from the centre out — the picture never moves or scales, the opening just uncovers it. The two modes are different pictures at the same height, so switching cold between them pops; `REVEAL_CROSSFADE` fades the stretched version out under the revealed one over the first 18% of step 2 to hide the handover (set it to 0 to see the raw switch).
- **Art trimmed 2026-09-16**: `Assets/Alien Interface1.jpg` (992x487) removes the black surround outside the glowing border; shipped as `textures/alien-interface-trimmed.jpg` under a NEW filename on purpose (the browser image cache has already cost time on this project once when a texture was replaced in place). Trimming changed the aspect 1.962 -> 2.037, so at the same baked width the rect is 860 x 422 (y 101-523) rather than 860 x 438 — but the GLOWING FRAME itself now renders slightly BIGGER than before (849x419 on screen vs 824x406), since the black that used to inset it is gone. Close enough to "the same area" that the settings were kept as-is; if it ever needs to be pixel-identical to the untrimmed version, drawing at width ~834 centred near y~316 is the equivalent.
- **Open on the alien interface specifically**: any trigger other than the Play button; how the screen LEAVES again (shrink back? cut?); the screen's own content changing (glyphs/values); the thin black edge still just outside the glowing border in the trimmed art (harmless on the dark screen, but it IS a hard rectangle on cardboard if that ever reads wrong).
- **Growth timing re-tuned 2026-09-16**: Luke set new slider values live (start height 5, widen 0.6s, hold 0.05s, open 0.6s) — now `alienInterfaceProto.js`'s DEFAULTS (sliders still live there; only the final size was asked to be locked, not this timing).
- **Added 2026-09-16, lowered into the game** (`app/alien-lower.html` + `app/src/alienLowerProto.js`): the full moment Luke described — "when a player has picked up the Alien device and presses the button to choose their target, I want this UI to be lowered down from the top of the screen... fill most of the screen, and should be in front of anything else." A full-viewport prototype: a placeholder game scene behind a "Use alien device" button; pressing it lowers the cardboard panel on two strings from off-screen, then auto-plays the alien-interface growth once it settles. New art: `Assets/String.png` -> `textures/hanging-string.png`, a ~13px-wide twisted cord that happened to already match the panel's own built-in string-stub width almost exactly (no rescale needed) — tiled vertically to continue it. String length is a large FIXED constant (2400, ~4.4 panel-heights) rather than a tracked anchor point, which is enough by construction (not just luck) to keep the string's top off-screen at any fill%/top-margin the sliders can produce, at any viewport aspect — see the file's own header for the inequality. Sliders: lower duration, fill %, top margin. Growth timing/final-size constants are DUPLICATED from `alienInterfaceProto.js` (that file runs against its own DOM as a load-time side effect, not set up to be imported as a library — same reasoning `dialProto.js` was left alone for) — if they're ever retuned, both files need updating, or this is the moment to factor the growth maths into a shared module.
- **Open on the lowered-in staging specifically**: it's a button standing in for the real trigger (opening the target-picking menu is still separate work — see the "Paused 2026-09-15" abduction-targeting entry, not yet connected to any of this); how the panel leaves again; a real game scene behind it instead of the placeholder gradient; resize mid-animation snaps rather than re-easing.
- **Added 2026-09-17, the target picker's content** (`app/alien-target-picker.html` + `app/src/alienTargetPickerProto.js`): the avatar + name tag + left/right arrows shown on the grown screen, per Luke's mockup. Three real ambiguities in the ask were resolved by asking rather than guessing (Luke's own answers): this pass is arrows + avatar + name tag + working cycling ONLY — no team-name box, no confirm button ("added soon"); no shimmer-in/rotate animation yet ("next step"); the arrows cycle within ONE fixed stand-in roster, not a cross-team combined list — team-switching will eventually be "clicking on the team name window," not built yet. Stand-in roster: the lobby's own Dev-player names paired with real character art from skyPath.js's ROSTER (`indy` first, since it's what Luke's mockup happened to show).
- **Both pieces are the REAL in-game rendering, not lookalikes**: the avatar is the exact `figure-<key>` art the game itself uses — its coloured oval backing isn't a separate recolourable layer (checked: the real game only tints a NAME TAG's glow per player, never the card) — it's baked into each character's own art already. The name tag calls `skypath/nameTag.js`'s `buildNameTagCanvas` directly, the actual renderer, not a copy. Real bug hit doing this: that function's own `glowColor` default parameter (`GLOW_COLOR_DEFAULT`) is a dangling reference to a constant that doesn't exist in that file — the real game's own `attachNameTag` already always passes an explicit colour for exactly this reason, and now so does this. Worth knowing if `buildNameTagCanvas` is ever called from a fourth place.
- **Placement is a first-pass guess, not a measurement** — Luke's mockup was pasted inline in chat, not a file this could sample pixels from, so avatar/name-tag/arrow size and position are all sliders here (unlike the growth screen's own baked final size) pending a real side-by-side tuning pass.
- **The growth-constants duplication is now THREE copies** (`alienInterfaceProto.js`, `alienLowerProto.js`, this file) — flagging again that consolidating into a shared module is worth doing soon, still not done unasked.
- **Open**: everything in "NOT BUILT YET" above — the shimmer-in/rotate animation, the team box + click-to-switch-teams, the confirm button, real cross-team data in place of the stand-in roster, and connecting any of this to the real abduction-targeting feature (see the "Paused 2026-09-15" entry).
- **Placement baked in 2026-09-17** (`alienTargetPickerProto.js`): avatar 224/y343, name tag 49/y189, arrows 114/y350/inset267 — Luke's own tuned values, no longer sliders.
- **Avatar spin added**: continuous rotation about the avatar's own vertical axis (name tag explicitly excluded — "not the name"). Faked in 2D the same way the dial's turn already relies on a real fact: the in-game card is a single FRONT-only texture rendered double-sided, so a true 3D Y-rotation shows a mirrored front once past 90° anyway — `ctx.scale(cos(angle), 1)` reproduces exactly that in Canvas2D, squashing to a sliver and flipping negative, which draws as a mirror for free.
- **Added 2026-09-17, six appearance-effect candidates for Luke to choose from** (`app/alien-fizzle-tuner.html` + `app/src/alienFizzleProto.js`), per his ask: "a very quick animation with a green sci-fi static fizzle that starts at the bottom and works its way up... show me six different prototypes... I'll pick the best one." Deliberately NOT integrated into the target picker — this page only compares them. All six: bottom-to-top, green, ~0.5s (tunable). They differ in how the boundary reads: (1) hard wipe + flickering static band, (2) glitch slices (VHS-tracking-error look), (3) full-avatar scanline sweep fading out as it finishes + bright edge line, (4) particle dissolve (density-graded green dots, no hard edge), (5) CRT flicker bars + sideways jitter that settles out, (6) a fixed cell grid with per-cell reveal thresholds biased toward the bottom but random enough to read as a ragged, organic edge rather than a ruler-straight line, each cell flashing green the instant it reveals.
- **Real bug caught before shipping**: variant 6's bottom-bias was inverted at first (`fracFromBottom` used `ry` — 0 at the TOP row — backwards), so cells lit up scattered all over rather than bottom-first; caught by rendering an actual mid-animation frame (captured via `canvas.toDataURL()` synchronously inside the same script that triggered the play, not a separate screenshot call afterward — a separate call's own round-trip latency was long enough for a 3s test animation to finish before the screenshot fired, which read as "showing the fully-revealed avatar instantly" and would have been a false pass). Fixed and re-verified the same way.
- **Open**: which variant Luke picks (none are wired in yet); how the fizzle and the ambient spin above are meant to interact (play the fizzle then start spinning, or reveal WHILE already spinning?); a real trigger instead of a button; this is a FOURTH copy of the growth-screen constants now (`alienInterfaceProto.js`, `alienLowerProto.js`, `alienTargetPickerProto.js`, this file) — genuinely worth a shared module at this point.

### ✓ Built 2026-09-18: the cardboard UI, inserted into the real game as the abduction targeting picker
**Read `skyPath.js`'s own "abduction cardboard UI" section header first (search for that heading) — full design notes live there, this is only a pointer.** Replaces the plain-HTML `#abductMenu` list (round 2 of the original abduction-targeting work, "Paused 2026-09-15" entry above) with the actual cardboard UI built across the prototypes in the entries above this one — Luke: "It's time to insert this into the game as the interface when a player activates the alien abduction device. Include a placeholder team choice window and the arrows to choose the target member, and a confirmation button. The whole process doesn't have to be complete at this stage."
- **Glitch slices (variant 2 of the six in `alienFizzleProto.js`) is the one Luke picked**, at a locked 0.30s. If the arrows are pressed again before a reveal finishes, it restarts from t=0 for the newly-selected target immediately — Luke: "if the player presses the button before the animation is complete, interrupt and move to the next player" — deliberately no "ignore while animating" guard, the opposite of the dial's own turn.
- **New shared module** `app/src/alienInterfaceCore.js` — the growth constants/maths that had been duplicated FOUR times across the prototypes (their own headers each flagged this) finally got a home, since a 5th copy (the real game) was one too many. The four prototype files are UNCHANGED, still carrying their own copies — repointing them at this module is a pure no-behaviour-change refactor, available whenever it's wanted, not done as part of this change.
- **A real, known gap, not silently faked**: a target's avatar always shows as the `ghost` character, and their name tag always glows the same warm gold — this device has no way to know another team's chosen character/colour at all (player-state broadcasts carrying that are filtered to the sender's own group in `useLobby.js`; `getAbductionTargets()` only ever carried token/displayName/groupId/island). Fixing this for real means broadcasting character/colour session-wide, a `useLobby.js` change — not something worth faking with a made-up character here.
- **Explicitly deferred, per "doesn't have to be complete"**: clicking the team box to switch teams (Luke's own words for the eventual mechanism: "Switch Team can be simply clicking on the team name window, for now" — currently a placeholder with no click behaviour); how the panel leaves again after Confirm/Cancel (currently just hides instantly, no reverse animation); a real exit animation on Confirm.
- **Real bug found and fixed before calling this done**: the team-box/confirm-button (plain DOM elements laid over the canvas) were only positioned ONCE, at the moment the screen finished growing — the canvas itself re-lays-out every frame (so it always tracks the current window size), but the two buttons didn't, so resizing the window while the picker was already open left them drifted off the panel entirely. Fixed by repositioning them every frame too, same as the canvas; verified at three different viewport sizes including a mid-session resize.
- **Verified live (solo, via a temporary `window.__debugAbductTargets` override — see its own comment in `openAbductMenu()`; real cross-team data has no solo equivalent to test against)**: full sequence (lower → grow → glitch-reveal → spin) at multiple viewport sizes; arrow cycling; the interrupt-mid-reveal behaviour specifically (clicked an arrow 58ms into a 300ms reveal, confirmed it jumped straight to the next target at elapsed≈0 rather than finishing first); Confirm (closes, spends the item, sends the real `abduct-target` event); Cancel (closes, item kept); the empty-targets fallback (`showNotice`, cardboard UI never opens). `npm run build` clean throughout. **Not yet tested with a real second device/team** — the debug override is a stand-in for real cross-team data, same standing gap as everywhere else that's ever touched this feature.

### In progress 2026-09-19: real cross-team avatars, Earth + the confirmation dynamic — NOT FINISHED, still needs a real confirm button and animation tuning
**If Luke says "the alien device UI" or "the abduction target picker": start at `skyPath.js`'s own "abduction cardboard UI" section (same heading as the 2026-09-18 entry above — this is all still the same section, just grown). `?debugAbduct=1` (URL flag, works on any device including phone) auto-equips the abduction item and seeds two fake cross-team targets so this is reachable without a second real player — see DEBUG_ABDUCT's own header comment. It's good, but genuinely not done — see "Still open" at the bottom before assuming it's finished.**
- **Real cross-team character/colour, closing the gap the 2026-09-18 entry above flagged**: `useLobby.js` now records every player-state ping's `characterKey`/`colorHex` session-wide (`charByTokenRef`, not filtered to same-group like the teammate-rig handler is), and `getAbductionTargets()` hands both back per target. A target's avatar/name-tag-glow are the REAL thing now; `ghost`/gold is only the fallback for a target with no ping received yet this round (a brief window right at round start), not a permanent placeholder.
- **Target cluster (avatar/tag/arrows) moved bottom-left, Earth added bottom-right** (`ABDUCT_TARGET_OFFSET`, `ABDUCT_EARTH`) — room for the ship-formation animation, per Luke's mockup. Earth is clipped to the interface's own rect on purpose ("I want the border to cut the Earth, so that the bottom of the Earth is missing") and has its own top-to-bottom glitch-shimmer reveal, delayed 0.3s after the interface finishes growing (`ABDUCT_EARTH_REVEAL_DELAY`, `abductDrawEarthGlitch`).
- **The confirmation dynamic**: tapping the target's avatar toggles `abductSelected` (`abductToggleSelect`) — selecting highlights the avatar with two bracket images (`ABDUCT_SELECT_SRC`, one file, mirrored for the right side — see its own header for why there's only one file and why a mirror rather than a literal 180° turn) and starts a small ship looping between 5 fixed jump-cut positions near Earth (`ABDUCT_SHIP_POSITIONS`, `abductDrawShip` — deliberately NOT tweened, "a crude animation... shown in only five positions"). Deselects on a second avatar tap OR either arrow press (`abductStep` clears it) — no separate "cancel selection" control.
- **Two rounds of Luke's own taste corrections already folded in, not just first guesses**: an earlier pass grew/brightened the ship across its 5 stops and added a green glow behind it — both removed same-day ("I don't like the aura... remove it," "forget about changing brightness and size: have it at full size for all instances"). A separate earlier pass explored 6 drawn-line border styles for the avatar+Earth highlight (variants like corner-ticks/dashed/glow-pulse) — Luke rejected the whole approach in favour of the real bracket-image art described above ("I don't like the border options... skip the border around the planet"); if this is ever revisited, don't rebuild the 6-style approach without asking, it was tried and turned down.
- **All placement baked from a live `?abductTune=1` tuner** (three separate generations of it, each removed once its own numbers were logged — see git history if a future pass wants to see how): `ABDUCT_TARGET_OFFSET`/`ABDUCT_EARTH` (session start), `ABDUCT_SHIP_POSITION_DURATION`/`ABDUCT_SHIP_GAP_DURATION`/`ABDUCT_SHIP_PATH_ADJUST` (ship timing/path), `ABDUCT_SELECT_BRACKET` (bracket size/gap). The 5 ship positions themselves are still an ESTIMATE read off Luke's reference image proportionally, not a measurement — `ABDUCT_SHIP_PATH_ADJUST` lets the whole path be nudged/rescaled from Earth's centre without touching all 5 points individually if that estimate needs correcting.
- **Unrelated fix from the same session, same file**: guides/watchers couldn't pan their view — the drag-to-look pointer handlers were already wired up for every role, but only the active-player camera branch in `tick()` ever READ the resulting offset; `updateGuideCamera` (the guide/watcher's own separate camera state machine) never touched it. Fixed by hoisting the easing to run once regardless of camera branch, and folding the offset into `updateGuideCamera`'s own lerp TARGET (not added after the lerp, which would fight the lerp's own pull back toward the un-offset target every frame — see the code comment for the maths).
- **Still open — Luke, 2026-09-19: "It's pretty good, but not quite finished. We need to add a confirm button and tune the animation"**:
  - **A real confirm button for the confirmation dynamic itself.** The EXISTING `#abductConfirmBtn` is the older placeholder that immediately fires `chooseAbductTarget` regardless of selection state — it does not yet know about `abductSelected` at all. A reserved bottom-right spot in the interface art is already being kept clear for whatever this becomes (see `abductPositionOverlayButtons`'s own confirm-button placement math) — Luke specifically said not to build this yet ("We will then add a new confirm button, but don't worry about that yet"), so it's still a placeholder, not a bug.
  - **Animation tuning** — the 5 ship positions/path are an estimate (see above), and the whole confirmation-dynamic sequence (select → brackets appear → ship starts looping) has never been watched by Luke end-to-end at normal speed on a real device; expect another live-tuning pass once he does.
  - Real cross-team testing (two actual devices/teams, not the `?debugAbduct=1`/`window.__debugAbductTargets` stand-ins) — same standing gap as the 2026-09-18 entry above, still true.

### Added 2026-09-24: sky above the Lava Cavern's vent hole
Luke: "Right now, if the player looks up, they will see the hole at the top of the cavern, and nothing above it. I want to add some sky." Two source images (`Assets`, via Desktop): a base sky photo and a transparent cloud-streak layer, shipped as `textures/cavern-sky-base.jpg`/`cavern-sky-clouds.png` in `lavaCavern.js` (not `skyPath.js` — this is the cavern's own separate module, per its header's "make it a separate thing" decision).
- **Two flat, unlit `PlaneGeometry(1,1)` meshes** (not spheres/hemispheres) sitting high above the dome, scaled/positioned every frame from `domeRadius`/`domeHeight` — same "measure the loaded dome, don't hardcode it" pattern the dome loader itself already uses, so a future dome swap needs no change here. `DoubleSide` on both, since a flat plane's winding isn't worth getting right for something only ever seen from below through a hole.
- **Sizing math, not just a big-looking guess**: the vent hole (radius β‰ˆ 0.2Γ—domeRadius, from the dome's own 0.98 apex cut) is small and centred on the dome's axis, so any ray that actually threads it from inside the cavern is already constrained close to vertical — a grazing ray hits the dome wall first, never the hole. At Luke's own estimated worst-case 45Β°, the ray's horizontal reach past the axis comes to a few hundred units; `skySizeMult Γ— domeRadius` (default 12Γ—, β‰ˆ1800-unit radius on the 300-unit dome) clears that with a wide margin — verified live by dragging pitch to the 65Β° `LOOK_PITCH_UP_LIMIT` cap at both 1 and 4 spokes (the 4-spoke case puts the camera furthest out on the ring) and confirming no edge is visible either time.
- **Clouds tile and drift**: `RepeatWrapping` + `repeat.set(6,6)` on the cloud texture, with its `offset.x/y` advanced at two different small per-second rates in `tick()` so the drift reads as one direction rather than a mechanical diagonal. Transparent with `depthWrite: false` so it layers over the opaque base sky without depth-fighting it.
- **Kept as live TUNE sliders** (`sky height %`, `sky size Γ—dome`, `cloud drop %`, `cloud speed`) in the existing `attachBgTuner` panel, following CLAUDE.md's "tune it in the game" rule — no separate prototype page was built. Recomputed every frame rather than only on `buildWorld()`'s rebuild button, since it's just two cheap transforms and the sliders should respond immediately, same as `ringY()`/`world.position.y` already do.
- **Verified live** at `?cavern=1&spokes=1` and `?cavern=1&spokes=4`, dragging look-pitch to its cap in both: sky and drifting clouds visible through the hole with no seam or edge, zero console errors.
- **Bug found same day, and my first diagnosis of it was wrong**: Luke spotted a thin vertical line and a thick horizontal one crossing the visible sky. My first theory — the base sky's `repeat.set(2, 2)` tiling a non-seamless source — was disproven by Luke's own report that the lines persisted after that repeat was removed; he was right and I hadn't actually re-checked closely enough before claiming it fixed. Isolated properly with a diagnostic he asked for: swap the base layer for a flat solid colour (`0x0000ff`, no texture at all) and look again. The lines still showed β€” proof the base plane/geometry was never the cause, since it no longer had any image on it. That left only the cloud layer, still `repeat.set(6, 6)`-tiled throughout this whole test and not yet suspected. Luke checked the source art directly and found the real cause: "the clouds and tiling didn't go all the way to the edge of the image" β€” `Cavern Sky Clouds.png`'s content was inset from its own canvas edge, so every repeat boundary showed a real seam, nothing to do with viewing angle or the base layer at all.
- **Fixed** by reinstating the base sky exactly as before (one static, untiled copy β€” that part was always fine) and replacing the clouds source with `Cavern Clouds.png` β†’ `textures/cavern-clouds.png`, made to tile (content reaches the canvas edges; sampled border pixels are near-zero alpha all the way around, so even a remaining mismatch stays invisible). Confirmed seam-free live at 4 spokes, pitched up to the vent hole.

### Added 2026-09-24: glowing rim around the vent hole's lip
Luke: "add a glowing red line around the lip of the hole of the cavern... The line won't move, but I hope by changing the colours we can create an effect like slight movement." He asked first whether I could isolate the hole's rim myself rather than needing a separate Blender object, describing the method himself: "anything that touches a flat plane placed on top of the dome... extended slightly downward" β€” which is literally the standard "marching triangles" mesh-slice algorithm, so the answer was yes.
- **`trianglePlaneIntersection`/`chainSegmentsIntoLoops`/`sliceSmallestLoopAtHeight`** (new, in `lavaCavern.js`'s "rim glow" section): every dome triangle straddling a horizontal plane contributes the segment where the plane crosses it; segments are chained tip-to-tip by matching WORLD POSITION (not vertex index, so UV-seam duplicate vertices still connect) into closed loops; the smallest loop by radius is kept, since a slice near the dome's base would instead return its own much bigger outer cross-section. This traces the hole's REAL shape (confirmed live β€” the band visibly follows the hole's own slightly irregular, non-circular edge, not an idealized circle), from the actual loaded mesh, so a future dome swap needs no re-authoring.
- **Sliced just below the mesh's own topmost point**, not at it β€” exactly at the boundary is numerically degenerate (vertices sitting exactly on the plane). `TUNE.rimSliceFrac` (default 0.01 Γ— domeHeight) controls how far below; `TUNE.rimDropFrac` (default 0.05 Γ— domeHeight) is the separate "extended slightly downward" amount the resulting band then hangs from that slice line β€” both geometry-affecting, so both are TUNE sliders applied via a new "rebuild rim glow" action button rather than continuously, same pattern `spacing`/`islandsPerSpoke` already use.
- **"Changing colours" reads as movement without moving anything**: per-vertex brightness (a vertex-colour `BufferAttribute`, updated every frame β€” at most a couple hundred vertices, trivial) combines two sine waves at different spatial frequency AND speed, the same layering trick the lava shader's own `warp()` uses, so the glow travels around the ring unevenly rather than pulsing uniformly. `rimGlowSpeed` is a live slider (no rebuild needed, unlike the two geometry sliders).
- **Verified live** at both `?cavern=1&spokes=1` and `spokes=4`: the band hugs the hole's actual traced shape with no gaps or stray geometry, and the flicker's bright/dark patches visibly shift position frame to frame. Worked on the first real attempt β€” no failed slice, no degenerate loop, no wrong-loop selection.
- **Reworked same day** β€” Luke, after seeing the traced version in the real game: "the shape is completely wrong." Replaced the mesh-slice tracing entirely with a plain parametric ring (a circle, radius/height/thickness) per his ask for sliders to control exactly those three: `rimRadiusFrac`/`rimHeightFrac`/`rimThicknessFrac` (all Γ— domeRadius or domeHeight). No geometry rebuild needed for any of the three β€” the ring is built once at unit size and reshaped purely through the mesh's own `position`/`scale` every frame, same live-slider pattern the sky planes use, so all three respond immediately while dragging (unlike `spacing`/`islandsPerSpoke`, which still need the rebuild button for cost reasons that don't apply to a 64-segment ring). The mesh-slice utilities (`trianglePlaneIntersection`, `chainSegmentsIntoLoops`, `sliceSmallestLoopAtHeight`) were removed outright rather than kept dormant β€” see git history if that approach is ever wanted again.
- **Reworked AGAIN same day** β€” Luke: "Nope, that doesn't work. Please delete that entire addition; everything you added to the hole." Both code-generated shapes (the mesh-slice trace, then the parametric circle) are gone β€” the whole `TUNE.rim*` slider set, the ring-building code, `updateRimGlow`, and the tuner entries were removed outright, no dormant leftovers. Replaced with Luke's OWN art: `Assets/Rim.glb` (via Desktop) β†’ `models/rim.glb`, modelled by him directly against the same dome mesh this scene already loads β€” so, unlike the island/plank loaders (which measure and rescale because their own export scale isn't trustworthy), this one is loaded and added with NO rescale at all, trusting the glTF node's own baked-in transform. Worked on the first try: added as a child of `domeGroup` (itself untransformed), it landed exactly on the hole's real edge with zero adjustment, confirmed live at both 1 and 4 spokes.
- **Solid red for now, on purpose** β€” Luke: "make it solid red so I can easily see it and verify the position" β€” no texture, no colour animation yet. The two-sine flicker colour scheme from the rejected attempts (dark-red base, hot orange-red peak, two independent sine waves at different spatial frequency AND speed β€” the same layering the lava shader's own `warp()` uses) is NOT deleted-and-forgotten, just not wired up: Luke explicitly said to "remember how you make the colour scheme" for reuse once this shape is confirmed in place. Needs a per-vertex phase (e.g. angle around the ring, derivable from the loaded mesh's own vertex positions) and a vertex-colour attribute updated every frame the same way as before.
- **Colour wired up same day, placement confirmed**: `rim.glb`'s loader now computes each vertex's own `phase` as `atan2` of its WORLD x/z (not local β€” robust to whatever origin/rotation the export happens to use), builds a per-mesh vertex-colour attribute, and `updateRimGlow(t)` (called from `tick()`) drives it with the same two-sine mix as the earlier rejected attempts. `rimGlowSpeed` is a live TUNE slider. Verified live: the glow now traces the hole's REAL shape (this is Luke's own modelled art, not a derived/traced outline) and the bright/dark flicker visibly shifts position frame to frame.
- **Added `rimVerticalScale`** β€” Luke: "it's not very noticeable... add a slider to increase only the vertical dimension." First attempt used `scale.y` on `rim.glb`'s own ROOT object β€” Luke: "all values above 1 are invisible." Real bug: `Object3D.scale` also scales that node's own TRANSLATION, not just its geometry; since the mesh sits far from the scene origin (out at the dome's own radius/height), scaling its parent by Y flung the whole ring proportionally farther away every frame instead of thickening it β€” by 6Γ— it had been carried well out of view, which is exactly the "invisible above 1" Luke reported.
- **Fixed by stretching vertices directly instead of scaling a transform**: each mesh's LOCAL y per vertex is captured once at load (`baseY`), along with that mesh's own topmost vertex (`topY`) β€” the edge actually modelled against the hole's rim. Every frame, `newY = topY - (topY - baseY) * rimVerticalScale`, so the top edge never moves and only the drop below it grows β€” Luke: "it needs to extend downward." Slider range widened to 1-40Γ— (default raised to 4, up from the old 1) since "perhaps by more than your slider currently does." Verified live from 4Γ— up to 20Γ—: the top edge stays locked exactly to the hole's rim at every value, and the band visibly extends further down into the cavern rather than disappearing.
- **Still wrong at high values, and my own verification method was the reason I missed it**: Luke: "you're starting at a high position value and then changing to a low value and taking a screenshot: at a low value it looks right, but at a high value it's way off. The rim is sitting well below the actual rim." Root cause: `rim.glb` has more than one mesh (separate material slots), and the per-mesh anchor above used EACH mesh's OWN local topmost vertex β€” for a lower piece of a multi-mesh model, its own local top is not the model's real top edge, it's the seam it used to share with the piece above. Anchoring it to itself instead of the true rim meant that piece stretched away from its neighbour as the slider grew, tearing the assembly apart and dragging the visible band down.
- **Fixed properly**: the loader now finds ONE `topWorldY` β€” the true top, in WORLD space, across every mesh combined, not per mesh β€” then `updateRimGlow` does the whole stretch in world space per vertex (`baseWorld`, captured once at load) and converts each result back into that vertex's own mesh-local coordinates via a cached inverse matrix. Verified by dragging straight to 35Γ— from a fresh page load (the exact failure Luke described, not primed by first checking a low value): the top edge stays exactly on the hole's rim at that value too, band extending far down with no gap or drift.
- **Still wrong** β€” Luke: "Not fixed at all; even worse than before. But forget it, I've had enough of this. Revert back to the base thickness and delete all sliders." Whatever was still going wrong with the vertical-scale stretch was never root-caused a third time β€” the whole feature was abandoned rather than attempted again. `rimVerticalScale` (TUNE entry, slider, and all the world-space stretch code in `updateRimGlow`/the loader) was removed outright. `rim.glb` now renders at its own unmodified base thickness, no vertical adjustment of any kind.
- **`rimGlowSpeed` also de-slidered**, per the same instruction ("delete all sliders... Set rim glow speed to 2.0") β€” it's a plain constant (`RIM_GLOW_SPEED = 2.0`) now, not a TUNE entry, and no longer appears in the tuner panel. The "lava cavern" tuner has no rim-related controls left at all.

### Points system + victory stage (2026-09-25)
Luke's exact spec: 1pt per island reached via a CORRECT answer (a jetpack rescue earns nothing, since the fork it came from was wrong β€” `correctCount` already only increments on correct, so this needed no special-casing); 1 bonus point for reaching the final island with a jetpack still equipped; 0.5pt for obtaining either item (jetpack or alien device); 0.5pt per successful abduction resist. Guides receive the average of their team's scores, rounded to the nearest 0.5.
- **skyPath.js emits raw facts, not points** β€” per its own existing `emitRoundEnd` doc comment ("a game mode never accumulates or reads a running total itself"). New counters `itemsCollected`/`resistCount` (incremented in `resolvePickupClaim`/`resolveAbductPrompt`) and a `jetpackKeptAtFinish` boolean (checked at the success `emitRoundEnd(true)` call site) ride along with the existing `correctCount`/`totalForks`. `../lobby/scoring.js` (new) is the ONE place that turns those facts into points β€” shared by useLobby.js and the victory stage, so neither can drift from the other.
- **Cross-device scoreboard, closing a real gap**: `useLobby.js`'s `round-ended` handler previously only ever updated the REPORTING device's own `round.result` (confirmed by reading the handler itself) β€” meaning the guide, and every other player, never learned anyone else's outcome. Added `roundResultsByToken` (token β†’ result, aggregated unconditionally from every `round-ended` broadcast, same passive-table pattern `islandsRef`/`charByTokenRef` already use) and `teamComplete` (true once every roster token has reported). Reset on this device's own `game-started` so a stale entry can't satisfy a fresh round.
- **The guide's device now actually reaches a results screen at all** β€” previously it had none; `App.jsx` now checks `teamComplete` before the `'playing'/'failed'` branch, so the guide (whose canvas would otherwise just sit there forever) and every player (even one still waiting on teammates) all swap to the victory stage the instant the whole team is done.
- **Rebuilt as a real 3D scene, not a DOM/CSS panel** β€” Luke, after a first flat-HTML pass ("very basic... will be replaced soon with assets"): "this is a significant feature addition, and will require a new scene." New `src/victory/` module (`victoryStage.js` + `VictoryStage.jsx`, same split as `lavaCavern.js`/`LavaCavern.jsx`), built from Luke's own Blender art: `space-hemisphere.glb` (a starfield dome the camera always looks toward the centre of, so its rim is never in frame β€” same reasoning as the cavern's own sky dome), `victory-base.glb` (one team's flat cardboard platform β€” the eventual multi-team folding rig is explicit future work, not built), `victory-pedestal.glb` (one per seat, height = score).
- **Real bug caught by reading the glTF directly, not by eyeballing**: the hemisphere's own baked rotation faced its dome along a horizontal axis that didn't match this scene's camera (which has to look along βˆ’Z so the seat row reads left-to-right) β€” half the view showed stars, the other half showed nothing.
