Fixed with a `hemisphereYaw` tuner slider rather than a hunted-down constant, since the right value is specific to this exact export.
- **The pedestal pivot bug, caught before it was ever built wrong**: `victory-pedestal.glb` originally had its origin at its TOP (same class of bug `rim.glb`'s vertical-scale attempt hit β€” scaling a node moves its own translation, not just its geometry). Caught by reading the raw glTF node/accessor data directly and flagged BEFORE writing any animation code; Luke re-exported it with the pivot at the base ("I think I've fixed it"), confirmed the same way (no translation, local geometry now runs 0 β†’ 2.01). `scale.y` alone now grows it upward from a fixed floor.
- **Seat layout**: "positions should be even and uniform. Clean... do not read exact coordinates from the sketch." Every seat β€” all roster tokens plus the guide, appended last so it always lands rightmost ("guide position scales with team size") β€” evenly spaced and centred on X, alternating a fixed depth offset front/back on Z (the same idea island seating already uses for name-tag side, just Z instead of Y). Guide gets a pedestal + name tag only, no avatar (unchanged rule: "the guide should not have a character card"), and its own breakdown is a single "islands"-only value so it rises fully in the first (2s) stage and doesn't move again.
- **Reveal timing** ports directly from the original spec, just driven per-frame from elapsed time (`valueAt()`) instead of CSS transitions, since there's no DOM engine to lean on in a raw Three.js loop: islands over 2s eased ("starts faster... slows towards the end" β†’ `easeOutCubic`), 2s gaps between stages, items β†’ jetpack bonus β†’ resists each 1s.
- **Dev harness**: `?victory=1&players=N` mounts the stage directly against made-up scores, no lobby/Supabase/real round needed β€” same reasoning as `?solo=1`/`?cavern=1`.
- **Verified live** at 4 and 6 players: hemisphere fixed (full starfield, no gap), all seats render with correct avatars/name tags, pedestal heights settle in the correct relative order matching the scripted test scores (including the guide's averaged-and-rounded value), zero console errors.
- **Known follow-up, not yet fixed**: camera framing (`camDistance`/`seatSpacingX`) is fixed-size, not derived from seat count β€” at 6 players + guide in a narrow (portrait) viewport, the outermost two seats crop off-screen. Sliders already expose both values; a next pass could scale them from `seats.length` automatically instead of needing a manual retune per team size.
- **Explicitly out of scope, per Luke**: multiple teams / multiple bases, and the base's own three-part folding animation β€” "we will tackle soon," not attempted here.
- **Replay button** added (`#victoryReplay`) β€” just moves the reveal's own zero-point (`startTime = clock.getElapsedTime()`); `updateSeats()` already re-reads that every frame, so the whole 4-stage sequence replays from scratch with no extra state to reset.
- **Second pass, 2026-09-25 β€” camera, base orientation, pedestal rings, guide avatar**:
  - **Steeper angle + much closer**: Luke: "the angle needs to be raised a bit... raise it by 10 [Β°]", then separately "zoom in a lot more... until the base almost fills the screen." `camHeight`/`camDistance` scaled together to add ~10Β° to the look-down angle first, then both scaled down together again (preserving that angle) to close the distance β€” verified live, base now fills most of the frame with the seat row still fully on-screen.
  - **Base turned 90Β°**: Luke's own reference diagram showed the platform's long axis should run into the screen (depth), not across it (lateral) β€” the opposite of the first pass, since Base.glb's own baked scale is longer in X than Z. Fixed with a single `baseGroup.rotation.y = Math.PI/2` β€” the seats' own X/Z positions are independent world coordinates, not children of that group, so only `TUNE`'s spacing DEFAULTS needed updating to match (lateral spacing shrunk, depth stagger grown significantly).
  - **Pedestal outline rings**: a thin flat ring (`RingGeometry`) at each pedestal's own measured footprint radius, one fixed at the base and one tracking the rising top, colour toggled live between each player's own colour (matching their name tag glow) and pure blue β€” `outlineBlue`, a slider rather than a real checkbox since attachBgTuner's own `toggles` option is for mesh visibility, not an arbitrary boolean.
  - **The guide's own avatar, closing a real gap**: character-select already runs for a guide too, and its result was already being stored locally (`characterKey`/`pickedColorHex`) β€” it just never left the device, because `notifyPlayerState()` is deliberately withheld from the guide (see that call site's own 2026-09-13 comment: broadcasting it that way is exactly what once leaked a phantom guide rig into a live player's own game). Added a SEPARATE, narrower event, `'guide-character'`, sent once from `finishCharacterSelect()` when `role === 'guide'`; `useLobby.js` stores it straight into the same session-wide `charByTokenRef` table `getCharacter()`/`getAbductionTargets()` already read for everyone else, and deliberately returns before ever reaching `gameEventHandlerRef`/`updateTeammate()` β€” so the exact bug that comment warns about can't reoccur. **Not yet verified over a real multi-device round** (the `?victory=1` dev harness feeds fake data directly, bypassing character-select entirely) β€” worth a real playtest with a guide before calling this done.

### Third pass, 2026-09-25 β€” zoom, seat layout bug, guide avatar in the dev harness
- **Real bug found**: Luke: "there are several sliders at the top and I think maybe one of them is supposed to address this, but I can't see any change at all when I change their values" (seat spacing/depth stagger). Confirmed by reading the code: that block computed and applied every seat's X/Z ONCE, synchronously, right after `seats` was built β€” unlike every other tunable (camera, hemisphere), nothing ever re-read `TUNE` after that. Fixed by moving it into a new `updateSeatLayout()`, called every frame from `tick()` alongside `updateSeats()`/`updateCamera()` β€” the same pattern the other tunables already used, just not yet applied here.
- **Defaults updated to values Luke read off the live tuner himself**: `camDistance: 1.9`, `camHeight: 1.3`, `camLookHeight: 0.35` (kept as sliders regardless β€” "we may need to adjust them once other things change"), `seatDepthStagger` cut to 40% of its previous value (0.85 β†’ 0.34) per "reduce the distance between their depth and the front row's depth to 40% of what it is now."
- **Guide avatar wasn't actually broken β€” the DEV HARNESS just never fed it any data.** The real cross-device event from the previous pass was right; `?victory=1`'s made-up `characters` map in `App.jsx` simply had no `guide` entry, so `getCharacter(guideToken)` always came back null in this harness regardless of what the real event did. Added one, and the guide now correctly shows an avatar in the dev harness too β€” verified live at `?victory=1&players=5` (5 players + guide = Luke's requested "full team of 6"). The real cross-device path is still unverified over an actual round (same caveat as the entry above).
- **Known follow-up from seeing 6 seats for the first time**: at the new zoomed-in `camDistance: 1.9`, a 6-seat lineup crops two seats off-screen (same class of issue flagged for the very first pass, just resurfaced by zooming in). Not fixed here β€” flagging for Luke to weigh in on rather than guessing at a seat-count-aware auto-zoom unasked.

### Fourth pass, 2026-09-27 - per-section timing, guide scoring, rounding, player cap
- **Per-section score animations, decoupled from pedestal rise**: Refactored to calculate display values independently per section. Root cause of previous timing issue: both number display and pedestal height were driven by same continuously-advancing value. Solution: new `getCurrentStageInfo()`, `scoreValueInStage()`, and `accumulatedValueThroughStage()` helper functions in victoryStage.js (lines 156-207); both animations derive from same score breakdown but animate on different timelines. Each section's number finishes changing before its pedestal stops moving, controlled by `scoreFinishOffset` tuner (0.5-2.5s range, default 1.1s baked in at line 315).
- **Guide scoring now updates per-section**: Previously calculated once at start. Now recalculates each stage as per-category averages divided by roster.length (excluding guide), maintaining exact running totals internally while only display rounds. Lines 433-443 in victoryStage.js.
- **Rounding changed from Math.floor to Math.round**: Values 0.5 and above now round up (line 603 in victoryStage.js). Scoring.js also updated: items and resists changed from 0.5 to 1.0 point each (lines 23, 25).
- **Player count validation enforces 6-player maximum**: Roster capped at 5 regular players; guide appended makes 6 total. Validation at lines 278-282 in victoryStage.js with console warning.
- **Visual polish complete**: Section labels remain until replaced, label size reduced 30%, ring colors locked to player-top/black-base pattern (black only appears when pedestal rises), all section durations increased by 1s.

### Remaining work - victory stage (not yet started)
- **Multiple teams**: Current platform (victory-base.glb) single-team only. Need folding/rotating rig for 2-3 teams.
- **Full animation sequence**: Seats/pedestals rise, camera/platform animate in from off-screen, confetti/particles on complete. Only score reveal currently animates.
- **Wire into real game**: Only reachable via ?victory=1&players=N dev harness. Needs App.jsx integration and full round flow.

### Paper plane minigame, first rough prototype (2026-09-27)
Explicit standalone prototype, per Luke: "just build it as a standalone prototype" — he wants to judge fast whether the fold-then-throw loop can be fun at all before deciding whether it's worth building for real, so this is NOT wired into skyPath and doesn't follow the usual "build it in the game" rule (that rule was waived on purpose here, not overlooked).
- **Design discussion first, before any code**: Luke walked through the fold sequence in detail across several messages — 3 player-drawn straight-line swipes (centre crease, then one diagonal per half) on a sheet that stays visually flat while drawing, with several MORE folds happening automatically afterward (the wing-fold-down/spread-open steps) that the player never draws. Key point Luke was explicit about: a diagonal crease does NOT need to start at the "ideal" register point (e.g. exactly where the centre line meets the top edge) — whatever the player actually swipes should be "recognised and enacted" as a real, if imperfect, fold, not snapped to an ideal position.
- **Fold geometry**: `app/src/paperPlane/paperPlaneGen.js`. A crease is turned into a real fold by extending the drawn segment to a full line and clipping the CURRENT panel polygon against it via Sutherland-Hodgman half-plane clipping (`clipPolygonByLine`) — this is what lets an off-register line legitimately exit through a different edge than intended, per Luke's ask, rather than being forced back onto a "correct" line. The flap side is identified by a point-in-polygon test against a known corner reference, then mirror-reflected across the crease for the fold-down visual. Curviness in a swipe is deliberately NOT folded as a curve (not flat-foldable without real cloth sim) — instead the swipe's max deviation from its own straight chord is scored as "wobble" and only affects flight stability, never geometry. This whole approach (Luke's own idea prompt: "if you have an idea let me know") was proposed and confirmed with him via AskUserQuestion before writing any code.
- **Wing-fold and downstream folds use a FIXED proportion of the paper for this pass, not derived from the nose geometry.** Luke's actual preference is the derived version (wing position depends on how the nose creases turned out) with the fixed version only as a fallback for degenerate input — but he explicitly chose to sequence fixed-first for this prototype once effort was discussed: deriving it is more edge-case work (detecting when creases don't converge sanely and falling back), and he wants the go/no-go fun judgment fast. **Swapping in the derived rule (fixed kept as its fallback) is the natural next step once this loop is proven worth continuing.**
- **Flight physics**: simple vector model in the same file (`stepFlight`) — gravity, a lift term (∝ wing area × forward speed), quadratic drag, and a lateral curve term driven by wing-area asymmetry + centre-line offset (`curveBias`), plus the wobble-driven jitter. No wind in this pass (Luke: "leave it out for now, add later if things are going well").
- **Launch**: fixed throw power for now (Luke: swipe speed/length will set power later, but explicitly noted power alone won't determine distance even then — lift/angle/direction all still matter). Upward launch angle from a slider; left/right yaw from the throw swipe's horizontal deviation only (confirmed early: "swipe only determines left-right").
- **Target**: a flat circular island with a smaller hole at its centre, fixed distance/size, all exposed as tuner sliders. Landing classifies as hole / island (+ distance) / miss.
- **Real bug caught during testing, not guessed at**: the on-screen launch-angle slider panel sat in a DOM overlay at the bottom of the screen with default (blocking) `pointer-events`, covering exactly the screen region a player would naturally start an upward throw swipe from — so the swipe's `pointerdown` never reached the canvas underneath. Fixed with `pointer-events:none` on the wrapper and `pointer-events:auto` on just the slider input itself, so the panel stays usable but no longer blocks the throw gesture anywhere else. Caught by testing the real pointer-event path end-to-end (not just the underlying physics/geometry functions in isolation), confirmed fixed the same way.
- **Verified live** at `http://localhost:5181/paper-plane-proto.html`: full loop works end to end — 3 fold swipes produce a visibly plane-like folded shape with a real hinge-fold animation, angle slider + throw swipe launches it, flight arcs and lands, result screen shows hole/island/miss with distance, "Fold another plane" resets cleanly.
- **Known simplification, flagged not hidden**: the automatic downstream folds don't model the full real-world nested fold-over-fold sequence (flap-under-flap, true "fold in half then fold each wing down" nesting) — each side's wing is folded/opened independently via its own single hinge, which is visually close enough for a rough prototype but is a simplification worth knowing about if the derived-wing-position upgrade needs more geometric fidelity than this affords.
- **Not done, on purpose**: wind, swipe-speed-based throw power, redo on a bad fold line, and any trigger/integration into the real game — all explicitly deferred by Luke.

### Paper plane prototype, second pass (2026-09-27) — the first fold was actually broken
Luke caught this from a screenshot: the folded paper looked "cut into pieces" with thin disconnected slivers, which should never happen — paper folds, it doesn't get cut. He asked to stop, isolate just the first three player folds, verify those work before going further, add free-camera inspection, and make the crease lines actually visible (they weren't).
- **Two real bugs in `paperPlaneGen.js`, not a rendering quirk**: (1) the wing-fold step split each side's body polygon into "wing" and "spine" pieces, kept one, and threw the other away entirely (`void spineSide`) — that discarded region is exactly what showed up as a hole/gap in the paper. (2) the nose-flap mesh was being built from its ALREADY mirror-reflected shape (`flapReflected`) and then animated through a further 180° hinge rotation — mirroring it twice, which misaligned it from the body it's meant to fold onto. Both were found by reasoning through the actual polygon math, not by guessing at rendering settings.
- **Fix, and a deliberate scope cut**: per Luke's ask to verify the first three folds in isolation, the wing-fold/dihedral-spread step was stripped out of `buildPlaneRig` entirely for now (along with the fake cosmetic "keel" placeholder mesh that didn't match real geometry) — it now renders exactly two things per side: the full, uncut post-nose-fold body polygon (static) and the original corner flap (hinged 0→180° onto it, using the ORIGINAL unreflected polygon so the hinge's own rotation does the mirroring). The wing-fold math itself is still in the file for the physics/wingArea calculation, and the wing/spine keepSign convention was also fixed (it had wing and spine swapped) — but it is not rendered again until the nose-fold-only result is confirmed good.
- **Free camera inspection added**: after the two nose folds finish animating, the prototype now enters a new `'inspect'` phase — OrbitControls activates (drag to orbit, scroll to zoom) with a "Continue to throw" button — before proceeding to the angle/throw phase. This was a direct, explicit ask ("give me a button to allow me to move the camera around in 3D").
- **Crease lines made visible**: the guide line was a plain `THREE.Line`, which renders at a fixed ~1px on most platforms regardless of color/width — practically invisible against both the paper and the background. Replaced with `Line2`/`LineMaterial` (from three's examples/jsm/lines) for real pixel-width control, in a bright gold that reads clearly against both. All 3 creases now also stay drawn on the paper simultaneously as each is committed (previously only the currently-dragging line was ever shown, and even that vanished on release) — cleared only when the actual fold animation starts.
- **A second real "invisible wall" bug, found the same way as the launch-angle-panel one from pass one**: the tuner panel (`#ui`, top-left) was silently swallowing any fold swipe that passed over its screen region with default (blocking) `pointer-events` — confirmed by direct testing: the exact same swipe gesture worked when shifted a few pixels right of the panel's edge and silently did nothing when it started or ended inside it. Fixed the same way as the launch-angle panel: `pointer-events:none` on the panel, `pointer-events:auto` on its slider inputs only.
- **Verified live**: the two nose folds now produce a single, continuous, gapless polygon per side (confirmed visually — no slivers, no holes), the crease lines are clearly visible while drawing and stay visible together until folding starts, the inspect phase's orbit control visibly rotates the camera around the folded shape, and the full loop (fold → inspect → continue → aim → throw → flight → result → reset) still works end to end afterward.
- **Next step, when Luke's ready**: once the two-fold nose shape itself is confirmed to look right, reintroduce the wing-fold/dihedral step on top of this now-verified base — the wing/spine split math is already fixed and waiting, just not wired into the rig yet.

### Paper plane prototype, third pass (2026-09-27) — wobbly lines, free camera, step-by-step folding, third fold added
- **Crease lines now show the actual swiped path, not the straight chord**: Luke wanted the line to look "drawn as a continuous, imperfect, not-straight line, even if it is then interpreted as a straight line" for the fold math. Both the live drag guide and the persistent committed lines now render the full sampled `dragPath`, not just `[start, end]` — the fold geometry itself still only ever uses the chord (per the design agreed two passes ago), only the on-screen line changed.
- **Free camera during inspect**: `OrbitControls` now has `screenSpacePanning`, and permissive `minDistance`/`maxDistance`, so drag-to-orbit + right-drag-to-pan + scroll-to-zoom together give full freedom to view the plane from any position/angle, not just orbit around a fixed point. Explicitly a dev-only aid per Luke ("this probably won't remain in the game").
- **Step-by-step folding with a "Next fold" button**: `buildPlaneRig` (`paperPlaneGen.js`) now tags each step `auto: true/false`. The two nose-corner folds stay auto-playing back to back right after the 3 swipes (Luke: "the current setup is fine to regard as the first step"). Any step after that pauses in the inspect phase with a "Next fold" button instead of auto-continuing, so Luke can position the camera first and then trigger it — camera position is deliberately NOT reset between manual steps (only on first arrival at inspect), so repositioning sticks. "Continue to throw" only appears once no steps remain.
- **Third fold added — folding in half down the centre**: per Luke's spec, a new step folds the entire right half onto the left along the SAME centre line the player's first swipe defined (`plane.centerX`), rotating -PI where the two nose folds rotate +PI ("opposite direction of the two diagonal lines"). Implemented by nesting the right side's body mesh and its own nose-fold hinge inside a new outer hinge group centred on that line — nesting existing transforms, not new geometry — so folding it carries the already-folded nose flap along for free. Left side stays under a plain (non-hinged) group.
- **Verified live**: wobbly-path lines render correctly (code no longer collapses to `[start,end]`); orbiting/panning during inspect repositions the camera freely; clicking "Next fold" after manually orbiting plays the centre fold from exactly that camera angle (position confirmed preserved, not reset); the folded shape after all three steps is still one continuous, gapless piece; "Continue to throw" → aim → (loop continues as before) all still work.

### Paper plane prototype, fourth pass (2026-09-27) — player-drawn wing folds, replacing the fixed-ratio wing fold
Luke's spec: after the centre fold, view the plane side-on (spine at the bottom, matching his reference illustration exactly — a wedge with the centre line along the bottom edge and example creases roughly parallel to it, higher up). One player-drawn line creases and opens that wing to 90 degrees (perpendicular to the fuselage); the plane then visibly spins 180 degrees so the player can draw the second wing's line the same way.
- **Side-on view is a camera ROLL, not a model rotation**: the folded packet is still just a flat (near-zero-thickness) shape in the XY plane, so "turning it side-on" only needs `camera.up` swapped from the default `(0,1,0)` to `(-1,0,0)` while still looking along Z — this makes the spine (near `centerX`, previously reading as a vertical line) read as horizontal at the bottom of the screen, with no change at all needed to `pointerToUV`'s raycasting math, which stays valid for any camera orientation.
- **The 180 degree "spin" is a camera move, not an object rotation**: rather than rotating `rig.root` (which would require inverting that transform to correctly interpret the second swipe's coordinates), the camera itself animates from one side of the Z=0 plane to the other (`camera.position.z` from `+dist` to `-dist`) over ~0.8s. This is visually indistinguishable from "the plane spinning" to the player but keeps every coordinate system exactly as it already was.
- **New `LAYER_GAP` (0.003) Z-offset** on both sides' meshes, applied identically BEFORE the centre fold — since that fold rotates the right half -PI around the nose-tail (Y) axis, the rotation itself flips the right side's Z sign, so the two layers end up naturally separated (left at +gap, right at -gap) without this module ever having to reason about the flip itself.
- **Right side's swipe needs one negation, nothing else**: the centre fold already rotated the right half -PI around Y, which flips the sign of its local U (V is untouched, since Y is the rotation axis) — so a swipe drawn while viewing that side just has `u` negated (`toSideLocal()`) before being handed to the same `addWingFold()`/`foldPanel()` machinery used for the left side and for the original nose creases. No new geometry algorithm needed — third reuse of the same line-clipping approach.
- **New exported `addWingFold(sideInfo, lineA, lineB, toAngle, colorHex)`** in `paperPlaneGen.js`: splits a side's CURRENT body polygon by the player's line via the existing `foldPanel()`, replaces the body mesh's geometry with the kept "spine" remainder, and returns a new hinge step for the split-off "wing" piece to animate to `toAngle` (PI/2, both sides the same sign). `buildPlaneRig()` now also returns `sideMeshes` (mutable per-side body mesh + polygon + outer-edge U) specifically so this function has something to mutate after the initial build.
- **Verified with real vertex data, not just a screenshot**: an early visual check looked like the wing hadn't actually folded (still reading as a big flat shape from the front) — turned out to be a wrong assumption about which of the two overlapping layers was being looked at, not a bug. Confirmed properly by dumping the wing mesh's actual world-space vertex positions, which showed real, substantial Z-depth (up to ~0.7 units) after the fold — proving the 90 degree rotation is genuinely happening in 3D, not just visually coincidentally similar from one flattering angle. An angled/edge-on orbit view afterward also visibly showed the expected foreshortening.
- **Known limitation, not fixed here**: the wing-fold's rotation axis comes directly from the player's drawn line, so a line that isn't quite parallel to the spine gives an axis with some tilt away from the ideal nose-tail direction — meaning the "90 degrees perpendicular" is only exact for a perfectly-parallel line, and increasingly approximate the more off-axis the player draws. This is consistent with the project's established "imperfect input produces an imperfect but real fold" philosophy (same as the nose creases), not something to silently correct.
- **The old fixed-ratio `wingFoldRatio` tuner slider and `PLANE_DEFAULTS.wingFoldRatio`/`closedFoldDeg`/`dihedralDeg` are now dead weight** — the wing fold is fully player-driven, so these no longer do anything. Left in place for now rather than ripped out, since removing UI/config isn't the focus of this pass; worth cleaning up in a later pass.

### Paper plane prototype, fifth pass (2026-09-27) — stray-face bug fixed, throw orientation fixed
Luke caught two real bugs from actually flying a plane: a stray extra face reappearing after the wing folds (the "cut paper" issue from pass two, back in a new form), and the plane being thrown on its side instead of keel-down, wings-level, nose away from the viewer.
- **Stray face root cause**: the nose-corner flap from the FIRST fold is a separate mesh that stays attached to the spine when `addWingFold()` splits off a wing. If the player's wing crease happens to fall between the spine and where that flap already is, the flap now belongs on the wing side — but nothing moved it, so it stayed behind as a disconnected-looking shape once the wing rotated away. Fixed in `addWingFold()` (`paperPlaneGen.js`): after splitting, it checks whether the flap's own hinge point falls inside the newly-computed wing polygon (via the same `pointInPolygon` already used elsewhere) and, if so, reparents the flap's hinge onto the new wing hinge with `Object3D.attach()` (preserves world transform, so nothing visually jumps when it changes parents). Verified by deliberately drawing both wing creases very close to the nose tip (the scenario most likely to trigger it) and confirming the result stays one clean, continuous shape from multiple angles.
- **Throw orientation root cause**: the flight code only ever aligned the plane's nose with the velocity vector (`setFromUnitVectors`), which constrains facing but leaves ROLL completely undetermined — so which way was "up" was essentially arbitrary, not deliberately keel-down. Working out the real fix required re-deriving which local axis should map to which final-flight axis (Luke's spec: keel/centre-fold at the bottom, wings spread left-right, nose away from the viewer) — worked out on paper via the actual rotation math (a local axis permutation), not guessed at.
- **Fix has two parts**, both in `paperPlaneProto.js`: (1) a fixed `REST_FIX` quaternion, applied once to `rig.root` right as flight starts, that remaps the fold-frame's local axes (nose = local +Y, spine-away = local -X, wing-spread-after-its-own-fold = local -Z) onto a sane rest frame (up, forward, right). (2) `updateFlight()` now builds a proper forward+up+right ORTHONORMAL BASIS from the velocity vector each frame (not a single-vector align), which is what actually constrains roll — the plane's local "away from spine" direction now tracks world up as it flies, instead of spinning free. `rig.root` is now parented under a new `flightGroup` specifically so the fixed rest orientation and the dynamic per-frame flight orientation don't fight each other (the split mirrors what a `restQuaternion * dynamicQuaternion` composition needs to be clean).
- **Verified numerically, not just visually**: transformed the plane's local "away from spine" and "nose" directions fully into world space mid-flight and confirmed the away-from-spine direction reads as mostly world +Y (i.e. keel points down) and the nose direction matches the velocity vector almost exactly. Also confirmed visually — viewed from behind, the wings now clearly spread left-right with the keel a thin vertical sliver between them, matching Luke's reference illustration.

### Paper plane prototype, sixth pass (2026-09-27) — both wings folding the same way, not mirrored
Luke: "it seems both wings are being folded in the same direction, resulting in two left wings and no right wing." Wasn't visible in earlier testing because every test swipe happened to draw the centre line close to the true middle of the paper — this bug only shows up once `centerX` is meaningfully non-zero.
- **Real root cause was one level deeper than the swipe-conversion math**: `buildPlaneRig()` (`paperPlaneGen.js`) parented the right side's body/nose-fold content DIRECTLY onto `centerHinge.pivot` (which sits at world `(centerX, 0, 0)`) without compensating for that pivot's own offset — every OTHER hinge in this file (`attachAt`) correctly subtracts the pivot point before attaching a child, but this one direct `parent.add(bodyMesh)` never did. The two layers' spines (which must coincide — that's the entire point of folding in half) only actually lined up when `centerX` happened to be ~0; for any other centre-swipe position the whole right side was silently mispositioned in world space by `centerX`, which is what threw off which region a wing crease actually landed in.
- **Fixed at the source**: a new `rightContent` group, added to `centerHinge.pivot` with `position.x = -plane.centerX`, holds the right side's body/nose-fold content instead of attaching it straight to the pivot. `sideMeshes.right.group` now points at this group. Re-derived the matching `toSideLocal()` formula in `paperPlaneProto.js` to match: the correct inverse of "translate by centerX then rotate 180 about Y" is `u -> 2*centerX - u`, not the plain negation it had before (which was quietly assuming centerX was always 0 — exactly the same class of bug as the geometry fix, just one layer up).
- **Verified two ways**: (1) deliberately drew the initial centre swipe well off the true middle (`centerX ≈ 0.37`, paper half-width 1.0 — a large, deliberate offset) and confirmed the nose fold still produces one clean gapless shape as before (ruling out a regression). (2) Dumped the actual world-space vertices of both wing meshes after folding: left wing's non-axis vertices came out at Z ≈ +0.9 / +0.67, right wing's at Z ≈ −0.12 / −0.37 — opposite signs, confirming the two wings now genuinely mirror instead of both landing on the same side. Confirmed visually too, both from the inspect view and from behind during flight.

### Paper plane prototype, seventh pass (2026-09-27) — stopped guessing at orientation, built a diagnostic tool instead
Luke: "the orientation for both the folds and the flying is proving a persistent problem, and I'm not sure how to describe to you how to fix it." Rather than attempt another math-only fix, he asked for a tool that lets HIM show the correct orientation directly — click a face, rotate it, tell the tool (not just me) what's wrong — with throwing disabled entirely until folding/orientation is solid.
- **Scope cut, as asked**: `throwPlane`/`updateFlight`/`makeNoiseFn`/`REST_FIX`/`flightGroup` and the island/hole/ground meshes are gone from `paperPlaneProto.js`. Every tuning slider (nose-fold ratio, flight physics, target size/distance) is removed from the HTML — none of it applied to anything being diagnosed anyway. The flow now dead-ends at "plane fully folded, ready to hold for launch" with free orbit and no further action; the flight code itself still lives in `paperPlaneGen.js` for whenever this comes back.
- **Nose folds still auto-play, unpaused** — Luke confirmed that part already works and didn't want a pause added there. Diagnosis starts right after: no more automatic tweening for the centre fold or either wing fold. Both wing hinges now start and stay at 0° (flat) until manually rotated; nothing "helpfully" animates to a guessed target angle anymore.
- **Face selection**: `paperPlaneGen.js`'s `makeHinge()` now stamps `pivot.userData.hingeRef` with the hinge object and a human name ("Left wing fold", "Centre fold", etc.); every mesh gets a `userData.label`. Clicking a mesh in the new 'diag' phase raycasts against a live `selectable` list (grows as each wing fold is created) and walks up the clicked mesh's parent chain for the nearest tagged hinge — that's its "primary fold." A face with no hinge ancestor (the two body/spine pieces) is static: selectable and flaggable, but nothing to rotate.
- **Two rotation modes, as asked** — "locked to crease" (default, matches what Luke said he'd probably want more): a plain angle slider (−360°..360°) calling the hinge's own existing `setAngle()`, so it can only ever rotate around that fold's real crease axis. "Free rotate" (toggle): attaches Three.js's `TransformControls` (rotate mode, world-space rings) directly to the selected face's hinge pivot for arbitrary-axis rotation, with a live numeric readout (axis vector + angle in degrees, decomposed straight from the pivot's quaternion) so a result found by dragging the gizmo can be read off and typed into a real fix. Switching TO locked mode resets that hinge to a clean single-axis state (angle from the slider) rather than trying to preserve whatever free rotation was mid-exploration.
- **Flagging**: any selected face can be flagged as "wrong/extra" — turns it red (still visually distinct from the lighter selection tint if both apply at once) and adds it to a running, clickable list in the panel, so a stray face Luke spots can be pointed at precisely and stay marked even after clicking elsewhere.
- **A real click-vs-drag conflict, caught and fixed before it caused confusion**: `TransformControls` registers its own pointer listeners on the same canvas, and by the time a `pointerup` reaches this file's own listener, `TransformControls.dragging` has ALREADY been reset to `false` by its own mouseup handling — so checking it at pointerup can't distinguish "just clicked a gizmo handle" from "clicked empty space to deselect." Fixed by checking on pointerDOWN instead, raycasting against the gizmo's own visual object (`xform.getHelper()`) and remembering the result for the matching pointerup.
- **Verified live**: full sequence (3 swipes → auto nose folds → centre-fold/wing-fold diagnosis, each reachable and independently selectable → final "ready to launch" state, no throw) works with no console errors. Confirmed each piece of the tool directly: selecting a static face shows no rotation controls; selecting a hinged face shows a working angle slider that visibly rotates the correct piece in real time; toggling free rotation shows a gizmo whose readout exactly matches the locked slider's value at the moment of the switch, and dragging it updates the readout live; flagging turns a face red and lists it, unflagging reverts it; "Start over" cleanly resets to a blank sheet with no leftover state or console errors.

### Paper plane prototype, eighth pass (2026-09-27) — the diagnostic tool itself needed fixing, four real problems
Luke tried the tool from the seventh pass and found four things wrong with it, none of them nitpicks: creases vanished after the first three lines (including the centre fold, which he specifically called out as a regression — "this was OK as it was before"); the wing-fold camera read as looking down from above instead of side-on; "Free rotate" rotated around any world axis via a floating 3-axis gizmo, which wasn't what he asked for at all ("it was meant to be a way to show you how to actually fold the plane... rotating the paper about the axis defined by the folds I make"); and there was no way to ever reach an actual held-ready-to-launch view — the flow dead-ended in the face picker.
- **Every crease now stays visible, permanently, not just the first three**: new live indicator lines in `paperPlaneProto.js`, one per hinge, redrawn every frame from `hinge.pivot.localToWorld(hinge.axis * ±length)`. A point ON a rotation axis doesn't move when that hinge rotates, so each line stays correct automatically through any fold angle — no separate bookkeeping needed, and it stays correct through the final launch-pose re-orientation too, for the same reason. This directly restores the centre-fold line Luke said had disappeared, and is very likely what actually made the wing-fold camera hard to read (see below) — an ambiguous flat silhouette with no reference line for "here is the spine" is hard to orient yourself to, regardless of whether the camera itself is right.
- **Real bug found while adding those lines**: the centre-fold indicator was there all along, just invisible — hidden behind the opaque paper mesh, because (unlike the drawing guide line, which gets a deliberate +0.02 z-nudge) the crease indicators are computed directly from the hinge's own axis with no bias, so they sit exactly on the mesh surface and lose the z-fight. Fixed by setting `depthTest: false` and a high `renderOrder` on the crease-indicator material, so they always draw on top regardless of viewing angle — a bias-based fix (nudging along Z) wouldn't have worked here since which way is "toward camera" flips between the two wing-input viewpoints.
- **Investigated the "looking down from above" report directly**, not just patched around it: dumped the actual world coordinates of the camera, its target, the centre hinge's pivot, and the body mesh's bounding box for a real in-progress fold. The camera and crease-line math check out correctly — spine at lower screen-X as designed. What actually reads as "extending further down" than expected is the nose flap, which after its own 180 degree fold can genuinely overhang slightly past the true spine line if its crease had any tilt (confirmed: one flap corner landed at local x ≈ +0.25, past the x=0 spine). Real, and worth Luke knowing about, but not a camera bug — it's the same "imperfect input makes a genuinely imperfect fold" behavior the whole prototype is built on, and should read as far less confusing now that the true spine is drawn explicitly.
- **Rebuilt "Free rotate" from scratch around what Luke actually asked for**: removed `TransformControls` and the free-axis gizmo entirely. There is now only ONE rotation for any fold — always around that fold's own crease axis, always through the same `hinge.setAngle()` — and the toggle (renamed "Grab to fold") only arms or disarms a direct click-and-drag on the selected face itself in the 3D view, which drives that exact same angle by horizontal mouse movement. The angle slider and the direct drag always agree because they're the same underlying value; `makeHinge()` in `paperPlaneGen.js` now tracks its own `angleRad` so either input path, or a freshly-selected face, can read the current angle back without decomposing a quaternion.
- **Added the missing final step**: a fourth, no-further-button step ("Show launch pose") that applies a fixed axis-remap quaternion to `rig.root` — the same nose/spine/wing-spread → forward/up/right mapping the old (removed) throw code used, restated here as `LAUNCH_POSE` — so Luke can actually see the plane oriented as it would be held ready to throw. No physics, no flight, purely a static re-orientation plus a camera move; diagnostic tools (face select, fold sliders, grab-to-fold, flagging) all keep working normally on top of it, since child-hinge rotations are independent of the parent `rig.root`'s own orientation.
- **Known rough edge, not fixed**: the launch-pose camera's exact framing is a first guess (`position (5,3,-6)`, target world origin) and can leave part of the plane out of frame depending on what angles the wings were left at — free orbit/zoom still gets you a clear look, but the initial framing could use tuning once Luke's actually looking at a properly-folded plane through it.

### Paper plane prototype, ninth pass (2026-09-27) — crease overshoot and a silently-skipped centre fold
Luke tried the eighth pass and sent a screenshot marking, with a red box, crease lines poking out well past the top edge of the folded paper — pointing out the general rule: "Fold lines should only ever exist on the paper... the excess should be immediately and permanently deleted." He also noticed the centre fold, supposedly restored in the eighth pass, was still missing: the paper never actually folds in half before the wing-fold step.
- **Root cause of the overshoot**: the eighth pass's crease indicators drew a FIXED length (`paperH * 0.6`) symmetrically about each hinge's pivot, regardless of where on the crease the pivot actually sits. Every hinge in this rig is pivoted at one real END of its crease (not its middle), so a symmetric fixed-length line always shoots off past the paper on the side the crease doesn't extend — worse the further off-centre that end is.
- **Fix**: `makeHinge()` in `paperPlaneGen.js` now takes the crease's actual length (`creaseLen`, computed with a new `dist()` helper) and stores it as `hinge.creaseFar`. The indicator in `paperPlaneProto.js` is drawn strictly from the pivot itself (dist 0) to `pivot + axis*creaseFar` (the crease's real other end) — never past either real endpoint, for the nose folds, the centre fold, and the wing folds alike.
- **Same root cause, applied to input, not just display**: the wing-fold swipe itself was never clamped to the current panel the way the nose-fold swipes already are in `buildFoldedPlane` — a swipe landing off the panel would hinge and cut from a point that was never really on the paper. Added `clampToSidePanel()` in `paperPlaneProto.js`, mirroring the existing nose-fold clamp.
- **Centre fold's real bug**: its step was left as `auto: false` in `buildPlaneRig()` from an earlier pass, and nothing in the diagnostic flow ever plays a non-auto step from `rig.steps` (wing folds aren't in that array at all — they're built on demand from player swipes) — so it silently never ran. Changed to `auto: true`; it now plays automatically right after the two nose folds, before the player's wing-fold input, matching Luke's original design.
- **Verified live**: full sequence (3 swipes → nose folds + centre fold auto-play in order → left wing crease → spin → right wing crease → launch pose) now shows every crease line stopping exactly at its real endpoint with no overshoot at any step, and the centre fold visibly halves the paper before either wing-fold prompt appears. Face selection, the angle slider, and flagging all still work correctly on the resulting rig.

### The wikiHow-diagram detour (2026-09-27/28) — considered, not built
After the ninth pass, Luke went back to the classic 6-panel "How to Make a Paper Airplane" wikiHow diagram he'd originally described the project from, and asked whether building that exact fold sequence (rather than the free-form 3-swipe system above) would be simpler, since every crease in it runs centre-to-edge or edge-to-centre and the whole thing is symmetric.
- **What the diagram actually shows, precisely**: (1) fold in half lengthwise and unfold, leaving a centre crease; (2) fold each top corner down to the centre line (a "house/roof" point — this is as far as the 3-swipe system above ever got); (3) fold the RESULTING angled edges down to the centre line AGAIN, from the already-folded flap, producing a longer, sharper point — a second, nested fold per side that nothing built so far attempts; (4) fold the whole thing in half along the original centre crease; (5)-(6) fold each wing down along a line from the nose tip to a point on the trailing bottom edge, to roughly horizontal.
- **Diagnosis given at the time**: steps 1, 4, 5/6 map directly onto what already exists (centre crease, centre fold, single-line wing fold — the wing fold's line even gets simpler, since one end pins to the nose tip instead of being fully free). Steps 2+3 are real NEW complexity, not a simplification — a fold nested inside a fold, meaning a hinge parented to another hinge's flap, which hasn't been attempted here at all; errors would compound across two folds instead of one. The apparent simplicity of "everything is centre-to-edge" only pays off if those lines stop being arbitrary player swipes clipped by the generic Sutherland-Hodgman machinery and instead become closed-form formulas for a small fixed family of named cuts (corner→centreline-point, tip→trailing-edge-point) — that would retire the actual source of this project's bugs (the *generality* of "any line, any polygon, generic clip + generic reparent"), but is a different architecture from what's built, not an incremental change to it.
- **Luke's read on why this hasn't worked, independent of any one bug**: after nine passes of bugs being found, explained, and fixed one at a time, his conclusion was that the deeper problem is a shared-understanding gap about what shape the plane should be at each step — not any single defect. The diagnostic tool (seventh pass onward) was a direct attempt to close that gap by giving him hands-on controls instead of more verbal bug reports, and while it fixed its own real bugs (eighth/ninth passes), it hadn't yet actually closed the gap by the time this was written.
- **Not built.** Superseded by the decision below before any code was written for it.

### Paper plane folding — PAUSED (2026-09-28), decision and handoff notes
Luke's own assessment, verbatim in spirit: nine passes in, the fold mechanic has never really worked, through "some combination of I'm not capable of explaining how it should work, you are not capable of understanding, [and] it's just genuinely difficult to code it." Rather than continue trying to fix the general free-form system, he wants to go simpler still and pick this back up later — attention is moving to flight physics/aerodynamics next (see below), using whatever fold shape comes out of this simplification as the test subject, to see whether different plane *shapes* are even worth caring about before investing more in the folding UI itself.
- **The decision**: go back to exactly 3 player-drawn creases (centre, left diagonal, right diagonal — i.e. stop at the current nose-fold stage, no wing-fold swipes), then apply one or two folds Claude/the code chooses (not the player) to arrive at a finished, throwable shape. This deliberately drops player control over the wing shape for now, in exchange for something that reliably finishes in a recognizable plane. It's a narrower, more scripted version of what pass one already had (fixed-ratio wing fold) crossed with the wikiHow diagram's fixed geometry, rather than a continuation of the free-form clip-anything system passes 2-9 kept building on.
- **A second, related idea floated but not chosen (2026-09-27, previous message)**: reduce every player swipe to just the two X/Y coordinates where it crosses the paper's edge — no wobble, no curve, nothing else — as a simplification. Worth recording precisely because the diagnosis at the time was that this wouldn't actually help: the fold geometry has *always* only ever used the swipe's straight start/end chord (`buildFoldedPlane` in `paperPlaneGen.js`); the sampled wobbly path has only ever fed the flight-physics `wobble` score, never the fold shape. So this simplification is already effectively in place and was never the source of any of the nine passes' bugs — the actual recurring source was the *generality* of the clip-any-line/classify-flap-vs-body/reparent-across-hinges system, not curviness. Worth remembering so this exact idea isn't re-proposed later as if it were new ground.
- **What to actually reuse when this is picked back up** (all verified working, keep it): the Line2/LineMaterial pixel-width fix for visible creases; the crease-indicator technique (a point on a rotation axis is invariant under that axis's own rotation, so `hinge.pivot.localToWorld(hinge.axis * dist)` self-updates correctly through any fold state) and its exact-endpoint version from the ninth pass (`hinge.creaseFar`, not a guessed fixed length); the camera-roll trick for a "side-on, spine at bottom" view without touching model transforms (`camera.up.set(-1,0,0)`); the camera-position-flip "spin" instead of rotating the model; `Object3D.attach()` for reparenting a flap onto a new hinge without a visual jump; the `LAUNCH_POSE` static axis-remap quaternion for a held-ready-to-throw view; and the diagnostic-tool *concept* itself (click a face, rotate it around its own crease, flag what looks wrong) — even if the specific UI gets rebuilt around whatever new fold sequence is chosen.
- **What NOT to bring back as-is**: the fully generic Sutherland-Hodgman clip-by-arbitrary-line + point-in-polygon flap/body classification + generic cross-hinge reparenting system (`clipPolygonByLine`, `foldPanel`, `pointInPolygon`, the reparent-flap-into-wing-hinge logic in `addWingFold`) that passes 2 through 9 kept discovering new edge cases in. If player-controlled wing shaping comes back later, prefer expressing each allowed cut as a small closed-form function of one landing coordinate (per the wikiHow-diagram diagnosis above) over resurrecting the fully general clipper.
- **Files as they stand, paused in a working state**: `app/paper-plane-proto.html`, `app/src/paperPlaneProto.js`, `app/src/paperPlane/paperPlaneGen.js`. The diagnostic tool at `http://localhost:5181/paper-plane-proto.html` currently runs the full 3-swipe → nose folds → centre fold → 2 player wing folds → launch-pose flow correctly (ninth pass, verified). Flight/throw physics (`makeLaunchVelocity`, `stepFlight`, `classifyLanding`) and their tuning defaults still live in `paperPlaneGen.js`, unused by the current prototype but intact for reuse.
- **Not done, still open whenever this resumes**: the "3 creases + 1-2 scripted finishing folds" shape described in the decision above was NOT implemented before attention moved to aerodynamics — this is the concrete next step if/when folding comes back into focus, not something already tried and shelved.

### Paper plane — moving to aerodynamics/shape-comparison next (2026-09-28)
Per Luke: next focus is flight physics — getting different plane *shapes* flying in different, distinguishable ways — to find out whether plane-shape variety is worth the folding-UI investment at all before returning to it. This will likely use a small set of hand-built (not player-folded) test shapes rather than the folding tool, at least initially.

### Aerodynamics model — design discussion (2026-09-28)
Luke's spec: lift proportional to the plane's "vertical profile" (area as seen looking along gravity), drag to its "forward profile" (area looking along direction of travel), and a new lateral "wind" force to its "side profile" — all as simple foreshortened-area vectors, which he hoped would keep compute cheap. Worked through several real ambiguities before building:
- **Orientation over the flight arc**: agreed to have the plane's body simply ease (slerp, capped turn rate) toward facing its current GROUND velocity direction each frame, rather than simulating real rigid-body rotation (torque/inertia tensor) — Luke didn't want high complexity, and flat/near-2D objects are notoriously twitchy to stabilize in a real rigid-body sim. This alone produces the arcing "nose drops over the course of flight" behavior Luke described, for free, since the nose direction IS the flight-path direction.
- **Profile symmetry**: Luke's initial description (lift low climbing steeply, high level, "increasing again" nose-down) was a mistake on his part — corrected to the standard symmetric picture (lift peaks at level flight, falls off toward either extreme). This needs no special-casing: the honest `Area * |dot(normal, axis)|` formula is already symmetric in pitch direction for free.
- **External wind**: rather than a bolted-on fourth force, wind is a constant world vector subtracted from the plane's velocity to get an "apparent" relative-air velocity, which is what actually drives lift/drag/side-force direction and magnitude — simpler than a separate channel and more physically coherent. Noted consequence, not yet revisited: since orientation tracks GROUND (not apparent-air) velocity, a steady crosswind's main effect is drift plus a transient turning moment right after launch, not a sustained sideways force — a real glider would weathervane into apparent wind instead; flagged as an easy flip later if this reads as too weak.
- **Occlusion between overlapping folded layers** (Luke's example: paper folded to 1/64th size should count for 1/64th the profile) — deliberately scoped OUT of real per-frame geometric occlusion (polygon-clipping along three independently-changing view axes every frame), since that's the same category of general clipping/classification machinery that caused nearly every bug across the nine folding passes. Recommended instead, for whenever folded (not hand-built) planes feed into this system: bake permanently-stacked layers (e.g. the centre fold's two fuselage halves) into one panel's area at construction time, and derive a cheap "how covered is this" factor directly from a flap's own already-tracked hinge angle (e.g. `sin(angle)`) rather than testing geometry at all. Not implemented yet since the current test planes (below) are hand-built with no overlapping layers to begin with.

### Aerodynamics prototype v1 — three hand-built test planes (2026-09-28)
Built per the design above, as a NEW standalone prototype (`app/aero-proto.html`, `app/src/aeroProto.js`) with its own supporting modules — deliberately not reusing the paused fold system's geometry pipeline, so this can be validated in isolation.
- **`app/src/paperPlane/planeShapes.js`**: three hand-authored (not player-folded) test planes — glider (span 1.1, 10° dihedral), middle (span 0.75, 35°), dart (span 0.4, 75°) — each built from fixed 3D vertices (a vertical keel triangle, a small forward-facing "nose" cross-section for a non-zero drag baseline, two mirrored wing quads). Equal total area (1.3 world units) across all three, solved via plain arithmetic on the hand-authored shapes — confirmed safe to do exactly, unlike the fold system, since there's no arbitrary-line clipping involved here at all. Dihedral (not wing shape) is what actually drives the lift difference in this model; span/chord are varied too, purely so "broad vs narrow" also reads correctly to the eye.
- **`app/src/paperPlane/aeroPhysics.js`**: implements the profile-area model exactly as designed above — `stepAeroFlight()` computes vertical/forward/lateral profiles from each panel's world normal (transformed by the rig root's current quaternion — panels never move relative to root, there's no folding/hinging in this v1), applies lift/drag/side accelerations, eases orientation toward the (ground) velocity direction at a capped turn rate, and integrates position.
- **`app/src/aeroProto.js`**: scene + UI wiring. Three planes spawn side by side, launchable together ("Launch all three") or individually, under shared sliders (launch angle, power, wind direction/strength, lift/drag/wind coefficients) so comparisons are apples-to-apples. Each plane gets a persistent Line2 trajectory trail and a live numeric readout (current profile areas, speed/height while flying; distance + flight time once landed).
- **A real bug caught and fixed during verification**: trails appeared completely invisible at first. Turned out to be `Line2`'s default `frustumCulled = true` combined with positions being rewritten wholesale every frame (not a fixed local shape) — the geometry itself was correct throughout (confirmed by dumping its actual attribute data and bounding sphere), it just wasn't being drawn. Fixed with `trailLine.frustumCulled = false`. A second apparent "still not visible" moment right after that fix turned out to not be a bug at all: the default camera looks almost exactly down the flight path, so the trail — genuinely ~5-6 units long — foreshortens to a short dash from that angle; confirmed correct by orbiting to a side view.
- **Also fixed**: the default camera framing initially put one of the three planes at a world-X position that rendered underneath the (opaque-looking) left control panel — not a rendering bug, just camera placement; confirmed empirically which screen direction corresponds to which world-X sign for this camera setup (non-obvious, noted in a code comment) and re-centered the default view so all three, and their full trails, sit clear of both side panels.
- **Verified live**: launching all three together under identical conditions shows the vertical-profile readout ordering exactly as designed (glider highest, dart lowest) and a consistent distance ordering (glider furthest, dart shortest) — though with the starting default coefficients the spread is fairly modest (~4% in distance under neutral wind), which is exactly what the exposed tuning sliders are for. Adding a 90°, strength-4 crosswind changed each plane's outcome differently (confirmed the dart's distance dropped noticeably more than the glider's), showing shape-dependent wind sensitivity is working, not just lift/drag.
- **Not done yet**: no ground/target beyond a flat plane (no island/hole scoring in this version — this is a pure shape-comparison tool, not the throw-a-hole-in-one game); no occlusion modeling (moot for these particular hand-built shapes, see above); wind is a full 3D relative-velocity effect but hasn't been tested at very high strengths or steep launch angles for edge-case stability.

### Aerodynamics — headless analysis tool + why v1 barely differentiated (2026-09-28)
Luke tested v1 and found the three designs barely differed under most conditions, and asked for the actual mechanism, not a guess — per his ask ("run the maths, much faster") built `app/scripts/aeroSim.mjs`, a headless script that imports the exact same `planeShapes.js`/`aeroPhysics.js` the graphical prototype uses and runs full flights thousands of times faster with no rendering. Kept as a permanent tool, not a one-off — reusable any time a "why does X happen" question comes up about this model. Run with `node scripts/aeroSim.mjs` from `app/`.
- **Root cause of weak differentiation, confirmed numerically, not assumed**: every wing/keel panel has a local normal with a mathematically zero component along whatever axis the plane's orientation is currently tracking (a rotation-invariant fact, not an approximation) — so ONLY the small fixed nose panel ever contributed to drag, and it was IDENTICAL across all three designs. The one force that could have told the designs apart didn't, by construction. Lift did differ (up to 3x between glider/dart) but was weak relative to gravity at default coefficients, so a big *relative* difference produced a small *outcome* difference.
- **Why glider barely beat middle, and why middle sometimes won at low launch angle / high lift**: v1 held wing area EQUAL across all three (see next entry — this itself turned out to be a spec mismatch), varying only dihedral, and lift scales with `cos(dihedral)` — cosine is nearly flat near 0°, so glider's 10° vs middle's 35° barely separates (ratio 1.2x) while dart's 75° falls off a cliff (ratio 3.8x vs glider). Separately, confirmed a genuine emergent effect: because lift here is applied straight up (not perpendicular to velocity, a known simplification) rather than costing anything via induced drag, too much lift can pump extra TOTAL speed into a climb, which feeds back into MORE drag (drag scales with total speed², not just the forward component) — so a floatier trajectory can actually average a LOWER forward speed than a flatter one and lose on distance despite more hang time. Also found a real instability: at `liftCoef=0.2` and a 60° launch, glider/middle's lift could exceed gravity during the climb and never come back down within a generous simulated time budget — no stall/max-AoA behavior exists yet to bound this.
- **Why the dart, airborne the shortest time, looked most wind-displaced**: distinguished "raw sideways distance" from "how much the flight path visibly bends" (deviation angle). Dart's raw lateral profile was actually HIGHER than glider's in v1 (steep dihedral presents more broadside area), but its absolute displacement came out similar-or-smaller because it also travels much less far forward — so the SAME sideways push is a bigger fraction of a shorter path, reading as more "blown off course" (12.3° vs glider's 10.8° in the test case) even though the raw numbers were closer than they looked.

### Aerodynamics v2 — real per-design tradeoffs, and a second real bug found immediately (2026-09-28)
Two things from Luke's next message: (1) a direct catch — v1 held total WING area equal across designs and only varied dihedral, which is NOT what was asked for ("a single piece of paper of fixed area arranged in different ways... a glider should have larger wings at the cost of less fuselage, and vice versa"); (2) a harder ask — make the three designs feel like genuinely different tools for different conditions (glider: goes furthest under good conditions; dart: shorter range but less wind-affected, in the air less time, easier to aim/straighter; meaningful glider-vs-middle gap), with explicit permission to make "big concessions on realism" to get there.
- **Fix for the direct catch**: `AERO_TEST_PLANES` now carries a per-design `wingAreaFraction` (glider 0.85, middle 0.55, dart 0.25) instead of a fixed shared wing area. Only TOTAL area (1.3, unchanged) is held fixed across designs, matching "one sheet of paper arranged differently." Dihedral spread was also widened and shifted (15°/40°/50°, from 10°/35°/75°) specifically so glider-vs-middle stops sitting on cosine's near-flat region.
- **First attempt at where the "freed" wing area should go was wrong, and caught by testing, not inspection**: routed dart's smaller-wings saving into a bigger KEEL (more fuselage, as literally requested). Re-ran the headless tool immediately after — this made dart's lateral/wind profile WORSE, not better (1.163 vs glider's 0.457), because the keel's normal is fixed dead sideways with no angle to soften it, so any area routed there converts to wind-catching area at 100%, unconditionally.
- **Second attempt, verified correct**: routed the freed area into the NOSE panel instead (dart's nose is now much bigger, middle's moderate, glider's small) — the nose's normal is fixed forward, so this converts into DRAG instead of sideways area, which independently helps two more of Luke's asks for free: a dart that doesn't go as far, and one whose trajectory is flatter/more ballistic (more drag + less lift == easier to predict/aim, less "glide"). Confirmed the nose needed a proportional (not fixed-width) shape so a much bigger nose still reads as a triangle rather than a wall — `NOSE_ASPECT`, width solved from area.
- **That immediately caused a NEW, more severe instability (found before showing Luke anything, via the headless tool)**: most angle/lift-coefficient combinations started never landing (NaN distances) once the nose was allowed to grow large. Root cause, confirmed directly: a panel's local normal only avoids contributing to an axis it's "not meant for" AT ONE SPECIFIC ATTITUDE — a flat nose panel has zero lift contribution at level flight, but once the plane pitches into a climb, that same panel's world normal swings toward vertical and starts acting as an unintended extra wing. This was always technically true even in v1, just negligible while the nose was small and fixed; growing it to carry a real drag budget made the leak dominant enough to destabilize the whole simulation.
- **Fix: panels now declare which profile(s) they're allowed to feed (`roles: ['lift'|'drag'|'lateral']`), rather than every panel silently dot-producting against every axis.** Keel is lateral-only, nose is drag-only, wings are all three (`aeroPhysics.js`'s `computeProfiles` now filters by role). This isn't a hack to paper over the instability — it's the physically correct fix: a fuselage cross-section incidentally facing upward mid-climb isn't generating real lift, so it shouldn't count as if it were. Re-ran the full headless sweep afterward: zero NaN/runaway results across every angle/power/coefficient combination tested, including the ones that broke before.
- **Verified the actual goals are now met, numerically** (`node scripts/aeroSim.mjs`, default coefficients, angle 45): glider 13.51 vs middle 12.28 vs dart 11.34 — a real ~9-16% spread at DEFAULT settings (v1 was ~2-4%). At max lift coefficient the spread widens to glider 39% ahead of middle, 74% ahead of dart. Dart's raw lateral profile (0.459) is now correctly the LOWEST of the three (was the highest in v1), and its absolute wind displacement is also lowest, though its deviation ANGLE is still highest — flagged as an honest residual tension (see next line), not something forced away.
- **Known residual tension, flagged rather than papered over**: dart's flight PATH still bends by the largest angle under crosswind, purely because it travels so little distance forward that any fixed sideways drift is a bigger fraction of its (short) path — this is arguably still consistent with "dart is easier to blow off a short, direct course" but is a different claim than "dart has more sail area," and worth Luke confirming is the behavior he wants before further tuning chases it.
- **Verified visually too**: dart now renders as a solid, chunky arrowhead (dominated by its large nose panel) — reads as a clearly distinct "design", not just a smaller/thinner glider. Launching all three together now shows visibly separated flight paths and landing points, not near-lockstep as in v1.

### Wind coefficient bug — a self-cancelling formula, found from Luke's report (2026-09-28)
Luke: ran repeated flights at two different wind strengths, swept `windCoef` across its full range at each, and got results "as close to identical as I can tell" — strength visibly mattered, the coefficient didn't at all.
- **Root cause, confirmed with the headless tool before touching anything**: `aeroPhysics.js` computed the side force's driving term as `relVel.dot(axes.lateral)`. But `axes.lateral` is built perpendicular to `axes.forward`, and `axes.forward` IS `normalize(relVel)` by construction — so `relVel`'s own component along its own perpendicular axis is mathematically zero, always, regardless of wind. `windCoef` was faithfully multiplying an always-~0 quantity, which is exactly "does nothing, regardless of value."
- **Fix**: the term driving side force is now `wind.dot(axes.lateral)` — how much of the WIND ITSELF blows across the current direction of travel — which does NOT self-cancel, since the wind vector is generally not parallel to `relVel`.
- **Verified two ways**: the headless tool (`node scripts/aeroSim.mjs`) now shows lateral displacement scaling cleanly with `windCoef` at fixed wind strength (2.06 -> 2.73 -> 6.88 -> 13.6 across 0/0.02/0.1/0.2) where it was flat before; live in the prototype, dart's landing distance under a strong 90° crosswind went from ~11.3 (no wind) to 18.07 with `windCoef` maxed, visibly displaced sideways on screen.
- Luke's follow-up (2026-09-29): "I think we will likely go with a high wind coefficient. I don't see any reason to not just have wind strength producing a strong effect." Carried into the redesign below as the principle that wind should matter a lot.

### Island Throw: autonomous redesign of the whole minigame (2026-09-29)
Luke's brief (switched to Opus, asked for an autonomous attempt): players stand on the edge of the temple island once they reach it and throw planes at smaller islands nearby. The islands vary in distance and in height above or below the start, with wind shown by a neutral flag or windsock. Players read the conditions, choose a plane, and throw. It's a separate instance from players still on Sky Path, and no full world is built, just the same ground and cloud visuals. "Worry a lot less about realism... make it clean, with the plane types meaningfully different... be modular... build it with levers that can change how things work."

**Where it lives** (URL: `http://localhost:5181/throw-game.html`). Its own page by design: per the brief it IS a separate scene, not a stand-in for the game world. It reuses the real Sky Path assets throughout, rather than approximating them.
- `app/src/throwGame/flightModel.js`: the new physics, the plane stats and every lever.
- `app/src/throwGame/course.js`: the 7 challenges, the seeded random-course generator, collision and scoring. Pure maths, no meshes.
- `app/src/throwGame/skyBackdrop.js`: Sky Path's land/sea floor, curved sky, wind-cloud sheets (including the hole-punch shader) and far cloud decks. **COPIED from skyPath.js, not imported**: those builders are closures inside `mountSkyPath()`. **Follow-up if this ships:** extract them into one shared module that both use, rather than keeping two copies.
- `app/src/throwGame/windFlag.js`: the neutral flag.
- `app/src/throwGame/throwWorld.js`: temple island (`temple-island.glb`, Sky Path's own scaling), course islands (`island-basic-v2.glb` plus `island-circle` deck, measured the same way skyPath.js does), target rings and beacon, trails.
- `app/src/throwGameProto.js` plus `app/throw-game.html`: input, camera, UI, and the temporary levers panel.
- `app/scripts/throwSim.mjs`: headless validator. For every challenge x plane it brute-forces ~23k throws and reports hit counts and which plane each course favours. Accepts a lever-override JSON. Run with `node scripts/throwSim.mjs` from `app/`.
- `app/scripts/throwFindAim.mjs`: finds a bullseye aim for a challenge and plane. Used to confirm the browser game and the headless model agree exactly.

**The flight model: a point mass with explicit per-plane stats, NOT geometry-derived profiles.** Every attempt to tune the profile-area model (entries above) ran into a geometric side effect. Here each plane is speedMin/Max, lift, drag, windPush and guideSeconds, all directly tunable. Key decisions:
- **Lift is perpendicular to the airflow (default).** A force perpendicular to velocity does no work, so lift can bend a path but never add energy. The old "never lands at max lift" runaway is impossible by construction, not merely unlikely.
- **Luke's vertical-profile idea is kept as a lever**: lift scales with cos(flight-path angle)^n, so steep climbs and dives present less wing.
- **Swoop damping (`stability`)** was added after the validator showed an undamped phugoid. A glider thrown faster than its glide speed porpoised, wasting most of its launch energy, and its landing point jumped non-monotonically with tiny changes of pitch (e.g. 155 vs 200 vs 135 units across pitch -15/0/20). The fix adds `stability * dV/dt` to lift; linearising the glide puts the damping ratio at about stability/2.8. With it, glider range rises smoothly with power and pitch.
- **Wind "push" had a real bug, found by the validator**: the first version was a constant acceleration, so over a 12 s glider flight a modest tailwind added ~170 units of drift. Push now only acts until the plane's velocity along the wind matches the wind speed. That gives crosswinds a terminal drift speed, and tailwinds nothing once the plane outruns them.
- **`launchWithWind` (default on)**: a real but unhelpful finding from the stricter air-relative model. Throwing with a tailwind lowers airspeed at release, so the glider lost its zoom climb and landed SHORTER in a tailwind than in calm air (111 -> 93). The launch now adds the wind's component along the throw, so tailwinds always help. The crosswind component is deliberately not added, so sideways drift still depends on each plane's windPush.

**Plane identities (default stats), as they came out of tuning:**
- Dart: fastest launch (11-20), low lift, high drag, windPush 0.2, longest aim guide (1.6 s). Calm range ~50. Best for crosswinds, headwinds and high ground (reaching UP is a launch-energy problem, so the fastest throw climbs best).
- All-rounder: range ~72, windPush 0.55. Best in medium-range moderate crosswinds.
- Glider: slow launch (7-16), lift 0.06, glide ratio 12, windPush 1.0, shortest guide (0.45 s). Range ~115 and more with a tailwind. Best for far targets, useless in strong wind.
- Also found: the glider has a **glide floor**. With a high glide ratio it overshoots close, low targets, since it can't get down fast enough. That's kept as a real tradeoff; near-and-low is dart territory.

**Course design, validated rather than eyeballed.** Each challenge was positioned (reach limits, wind) until the headless validator confirmed its intended plane has the most hits: **7/7**. Hit counts per plane are the forgiveness measure. Glider-only courses are the hardest (73-207 hits of ~23k throws); open courses run ~4000.

**Lever robustness** (validator re-run under each alternative):
- Still 7/7: stability off, wind mode `air` only, wind mode `push` only, and profile effect off.
- `launchWithWind` off: 6/7 (the tailwind course breaks, as predicted).
- Vertical lift with a cap below 1 g: 5/7. The glider's range falls to 85, so the far courses would need moving.

**UI and flow**:
- Aim with the mouse: x sets bearing ±45°, y sets pitch -25..70°.
- Hold the left button (or Space) to charge power. The power meter can fill or ping-pong, as a lever.
- Release to throw. Right-drag orbits the view, the wheel zooms, R resets it.
- Choose a plane with the cards or 1/2/3. Each card's bars (range, wind grip, aim guide) are computed from the live stats, so they stay honest while tuning.
- The aim guide is a dashed predicted path, its length set per plane, and it ignores wind by default so the flag has to be read.
- Chase camera during flight (a lever: chase or fixed). Persistent coloured trails and landing markers.
- Scoring: 3 points in the inner quarter, 2 within 60% of the radius, 1 on the deck, with best-per-course and a challenge total.
- Random courses are seeded.
- The cloud sheets drift WITH the course's wind: a second wind cue, and a lever.
- Time of day is a lever. It defaults to dusk, since Sky Path's day cycle reaches the temple at sunP = 1.
- "Copy settings" puts the full lever and stat JSON on the clipboard for baking in.

**Verified**:
- The page loads with Sky Path's real sky, clouds, floor, temple-island paving and island models.
- The flag hangs limp on the calm course and streams flat out in the 6.5 crosswind.
- The game and the headless model agree exactly: "First throw" bullseye 0.8 from centre in 1.6 s in both, and "The far island" glider bullseye 0.1 from centre in 7.6 s in both.
- The levers panel builds 35 controls that apply live.
- Fixed during verification: the plane in hand covered the target (camera moved up-left), the flag stood in front of targets (moved to the left edge), and the controls hint overlapped the course panel (moved above the aim readout, and it now fades after the first throw).

**Not done / open**:
- No player figure: the plane is shown at hand height.
- No sound.
- Island collision uses a disc-plus-cone approximation of the real mesh. It's fine at gameplay distances, but it's not the mesh.
- The temple billboard itself isn't placed, only its island underfoot, since the player faces away from it.
- (Superseded the same day. See "Island Throw: input redesign" below: aim-with-the-mouse and hold-to-charge were replaced.)
- Latent bug in the SUPERSEDED aero prototype, noticed while writing this one's orientation code: `aeroPhysics.js`'s `basisQuaternionFromForward` builds a basis `(cross(forward, up), up, forward)` with determinant -1. That's a reflection, so the quaternion it produces is not a proper rotation, and panel normals in `aero-proto.html` may have been subtly wrong. Moot unless that model is revived; the new game uses `orientAlong()` in throwGameProto.js, which builds a proper rotation.

### Island Throw: input redesign, angle sweep + swipe (2026-09-29)
Luke on the first version: "you've got the player selecting their destination by placing a 3D coordinate. This is too easy, as it does too much of the work for the player." Wanted: upward angle as ONE decision made before launch, by pressing while a value rises and falls ("so that it's hard to choose exactly the value desired"). Lateral direction and power as a SEPARATE decision at launch, by swiping: direction = left/right, length = power.
- **Phase flow** (`throwGameProto.js`): `angle` (a needle sweeps angleMin..angleMax; tap/click/Space locks it) -> `swipe` (waiting for the swipe) -> `swiping` (press-drag, with yaw and power following the pointer live) -> release throws -> `flying` -> `result`.
  - A tap with no movement in the swipe phase does nothing, so double-tapping the angle can't fire a zero-power throw.
  - A swipe that doesn't go up the screen isn't a throw, and toasts "Swipe away from you".
  - "Re-pick angle" (button, Backspace or A) goes back to the sweep.
  - Works with mouse drag or touch: OrbitControls' one-finger touch is disabled so a swipe can't spin the camera; look-around is two fingers.
- **Swipe mapping**: direction measured from straight up the screen maps 1:1 onto throw direction (clamped ±maxYawDeg). Length / (swipeFullPower x the screen's shorter side) = power. That's length only, not speed, as asked.
- **Angle gauge**: a side-on protractor on the right (the left of the view belongs to the flag). It shows the live value and turns gold with "locked". The plane in hand and the 3D arrow also tilt with the sweep.
- **New input levers** (panel section "Input"): sweep time (1.2 s min->max default), sweep shape (triangle = constant speed; sine = lingers at the ends, making extremes easier than middle angles), angle range (-10..70 default), swipe length for full power (0.45), and max left/right (50°). The old power-meter lever was removed, since power now comes from the swipe.
- **Guide default changed to 'arrow'** (launch direction only, and it grows with swipe power). The trajectory guides ('plane'/'full') are still levers but now draw only DURING the swipe. Left as the default, a live trajectory would let players slide the swipe until the line lands on the island, which does the same work Luke objected to. The card's "Aim guide" bar only shows in 'plane' mode.
- **Verified live with real pointer events**:
  - The needle sweeps (sampled 58 -> 69 -> 59 -> 27°), and a click locks it (52°).
  - A drag up-left read "23° left · Power 73%" mid-swipe, with the swipe line, power bar and arrow all tracking.
  - Release threw with yaw -23.2°, pitch 51.8°, power 0.73.
